- **Reported, not wired:** the solidity grader's G6/G7 (`hgSolGateMeasuredEdge`, read on OMNIROUTE and REVERSAL SNIPER cards) judges **expectancy**, and `hgFwdStats` carries no fill-aware expectancy — only the hit split — so a fill-aware G6 would need a new figure first (the hg-v955 rule: a field nothing reads is ornamental, and a reader with nothing to read is a claim).
- **No threshold moves.** The only verdict that can change is one a fill-aware sample at or over `FWD_MIN_JUDGE` overturns, and no crypto record written before hg-v981 carries one — so no board moves today; what changes is what the judge will read as those records settle.
- Tests: `tests/test-omniroute-fill-aware-judge.mjs` (30 assertions; **13/13** behavioural mutations caught across `hg-forward.js`, `omniroute.js` and `omnigold.js`, baseline verified green before every pass); `tests/test-forward-fill-model.mjs` follows the rule to its new home.

### The forward ledger's fill model never received a mark from the crypto desks that record, and most of them dated their records on the scan clock — hg-v981
- **hg-v980 forwarded the mark to the fill model from every gold desk and stopped at the gold list.** The crypto side has the same seam: `hgFwdSettleFill` needs **`mark`** (the price when the plan fired) to tell a resting order from a market one, and **CRYPTO SCAN, CRYPTOVERSE and 90PERCENT** carried it while **OMNIROUTE, OMNIPRESENT, SQUEEZE, OI FLOW, EDGE, REVERSAL SNIPER, BRAIN, the GATES tab, DEX SCREENER, TRENDMX, PINE (both lanes), CONTRACT REPORT, SUPER BEST, SUPER SNIPER and the inline CARD choke point** (SMC, ORDER BLOCKS, TRAP, DIV, COIL, APEX render through `cardHTML` and record there) **passed none** — and all but OMNIROUTE passed **no bar** either, so their records carried hg-v978's defect too: dated on the floor of the scan clock, the first bar of the trade skipped by settlement, one firing recorded twice across two clock minutes. EDGE decided **MARKET vs LIMIT on its own `sig.mark`** and never put that mark on the record.
- **What it cost, on the repo's own evidence:** OMNIROUTE prices **ENTRY at the setup level, not the last close** (hg-v424), so every plan it records is a **resting order**. In its committed replay (`scripts/backtest-omniroute-v531-results.json`) **4,353 plans opened, 1,495 expired unfilled (34.3%)**, and of the **2,832** that filled **not one filled on its signal bar** (`barsToFill ≥ 1` on every row; **1,459 — 51.5% — after the first bar**). The live ledger settled every one of those records as a **market order from the signal bar**, so a limit the tape never reached could record a target hit; `hgFwdStats` has counted `fillWins / fillLosses / fillUnfilled / fillUnprovable` since the model shipped and read **0 / 0** on every crypto pool. OMNIPRESENT is the other shape: TRIGGERED enters **at the live print** (8,426 of its 8,502 replay trades filled on bar one, 21 of 8,558 expired), a market-order desk that still needs the mark for the model to say so.
- **One reader, one home (hg-v949):** **`hgFwdLastBar(rows)`** in `hg-forward.js` reads the mark and the bar off the **last closed row** of the series a desk already holds — absent stays absent, never zero (the `+null` trap: a `null` close, a `0` time and a string close each yield `undefined` for that field, proved on every shape). Every crypto record map calls it or forwards the mark it already kept: OMNIROUTE the evaluator's own `livePx` (the decision bar's close, the same price `distAtr` was measured on); OMNIPRESENT stamps **`mark: livePx`** and the rejection bar on every candidate inside `opAssess`, so a TRIGGERED record's mark **is** its entry and an ARMED one rests away from it; SQUEEZE, OI FLOW, REVERSAL SNIPER, DEX SCREENER, SUPER SNIPER their own series; the GATES tab its survivor's own series, kept **beside** the state row rather than on it, because `window.engineState`'s survivor shape is a contract BRAIN reads and `tests/test-engine.mjs` pins exactly (the full suite caught the first cut putting the two fields on the row); EDGE `sig.mark` + its 4h rows; TRENDMX the mark `trendmxAttachMeta` already kept; PINE the signal's own close and rows; CONTRACT REPORT `planFrom`'s last close; SUPER BEST the pick's own mark; BRAIN the row's zero-fetch `boardMarkFor` (no series rides a BRAIN row, so **no bar is claimed there**, said rather than invented); the CARD choke point the mark its own geometry line reads (`hgMpMarkOf`) and the bar off `bookMeta.rows`; the BEST/SWING/SCALP publish spreads the candidate (its mark rides the spread) and reads the bar off its rows.
- **And the field is read:** the shared **KIND PERFORMANCE** panel (`hg-perf-panel.js`, mounted on OMNIROUTE, OMNIGOLD and NEW GOLD) prints **`fill N (W/L hit%) · U unfilled · P unprovable`** beside each mechanic's actual tally, and prints **nothing** while no record carries a fill resolution — a legacy log is not evidence about fills. The actual tally is untouched **by design**: the fill-aware figures are counted beside it, never instead of it.
- **Driven end to end:** the defect first, on an OMNIROUTE-shaped long limit whose tape never reached its level — no mark, a **target hit**; with the mark, `BUY_LIMIT` **unfilled** while the actual tally still reads t1; on a tape that does reach the level, **filled** and judged on the walk after the fill. OMNIPRESENT assessed headless stamps mark and bar on **ARMED and TRIGGERED alike**, the TRIGGERED mark equal to its entry. **24 resting records** through the real ledger and the real panel read **16 filled and settled, 8 never filled** beside an actual tally of **24 target hits** — and stripped of their marks the same panel says **nothing** about fills. **The census is derived by call shape and reads the record literal itself** (the first cut read a window around the call and passed SUPER BEST on a mark in a different literal 1,500 characters away): **28 writers, none bare**.
- **No gate is loosened, no threshold moves.** Records already written carry no mark and settle as they always did.
- Tests: `tests/test-crypto-ledger-fill-mark.mjs` (87 assertions; **32/32** behavioural mutations caught across `hg-forward.js`, `omnipresent.js`, `hg-perf-panel.js`, `omniroute.js`, `squeeze.js`, `oiflow.js`, `reversalsniper.js`, `edge.js`, `brain.js`, `engine.js`, `dex-screener.js`, `trendtable.js`, `pine.js`, `pine-sub.js`, `contract-report.js`, `super-best.js`, `super-sniper.js` and `index.html`, baseline verified green before every pass).

### The forward ledger's fill model never received a mark from eleven of the twelve gold desks that record — hg-v980
- **`hgFwdSettleFill` settles a record as if the order had to fill first** — a resting limit price never reached is `unfilled`, a fill bar that also touched an exit is `unprovable`, and only a filled order is judged — and it needs one field to tell a resting order from a market one: **`mark`**, the price when the plan fired. `hgFwdOrderType` returns null without it and the fill-aware pass stands aside. `hgFwdRecordScan` was fixed to forward it (its own comment records that no desk had ever had one) — and the record maps of **GOLD SCALP, GOLD SWING, SUPER GOLD, GOLD DIRECTION, GOLD ULTRA, GOLD PRO, OPTI GOLD, NEW GOLD, OMNIGOLD 1, TAURIC, 80PERCENT and GOLD PINE never passed one**. Only OMNIGOLD stamps a mark on its record. Found one field along from hg-v979's record maps, where `feed` went in beside a `mark` that was not there.
- **What it cost, on the repo's own evidence:** in GOLD SCALP's committed walk **561 of 2,605** plans (**21.5%**) are resting orders (285 BUY_LIMIT · 243 SELL_LIMIT · 24 BUY_STOP · 9 SELL_STOP) and **90 of 2,193** settled plans **never filled** — and in the live ledger every one of those was settled as a **market order**, so a limit could record a win it never opened for and a stop entry a loss it never opened for; `hgFwdStats` has counted the fill split (`fillWins / fillLosses / fillUnfilled / fillUnprovable`) since the model shipped and read **0 / 0** on every gold pool. The one reader that could act on it — OMNIGOLD's measured-edge judge prefers the fill-aware sample at `FWD_MIN_JUDGE` — was fed from OMNIGOLD's own records alone while the pooled GOLD SCALP and SUPER GOLD rows contributed nothing.
- **Every gold desk forwards the mark it already had:** the snapshot mark on the two home desks and SUPER GOLD (across the record maps that rebuild rows, the hg-v955 seam), the mint's mark on the picks GOLD DIRECTION and GOLD ULTRA borrow, the last close of the series the plan was composed on for GOLD PRO (its own note: *the bars ARE the mark*), TAURIC and 80PERCENT, the **break bar's close** on OPTI GOLD (whose orders rest at the range midpoint by construction), and the setup's own mark on NEW GOLD, OMNIGOLD 1 and GOLD PINE. **The normaliser refuses a mark at or below zero** — driving the rule showed it accepted `0` and `-1` as marks, the `+null` trap wearing a number. **And the field is read:** the gold desk note prints **FILL-AWARE: N filled and settled (W/L, hit%) · U never filled · P unprovable**, and prints nothing while no record carries a fill resolution — a legacy log is not evidence about fills.
- **Driven end to end:** the defect first, on a long limit whose tape never reached its entry — with no mark it is a **target hit**, with one it is `BUY_LIMIT`, **unfilled**, and the actual tally still reads t1, because the fill-aware pass is counted **beside** the actual rather than instead of it (by design; the panel says so). OPTI GOLD mounted headless records **four SELL_LIMIT** plans, every one resting away from its mark; the fill model settles **three** and sets **one** aside as never filled while the actual tally settles all four. GOLD SCALP's seven records carry the snapshot's own mark — and on the synthetic tape every candidate mints **at** the last close, so that desk reads seven market orders here; the resting case is OPTI GOLD's, said rather than implied. **The census is derived by call shape, aliases included:** thirteen gold writers, none bare.
- **No gate is loosened, no threshold moves.** The actual tally, the states and the R every record was settled with are untouched; what changes is that the fill-aware figures beside them are no longer empty.
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-ledger-fill-mark.mjs` (48 assertions; **23/23** behavioural mutations caught across `hg-forward.js`, `gold-forward-read.js`, `goldscalp.js`, `goldswing.js`, `super-gold.js`, `golddirection.js`, `goldultra.js`, `goldpro.js`, `optigold.js`, `newgold.js`, `omnigold1.js`, `tauric.js`, `eightypercent.js` and `goldpine.js`, baseline verified green before every pass).

### The forward ledger settled a gold record on whichever desk next handed it bars, and the gold desks do not read one feed — hg-v979
- **Every gold desk records `sym: 'XAUUSD'`, and `hgFwdSettle` matched on symbol and timeframe only** — so a record was settled by whichever desk next handed the ledger bars of that timeframe. GOLD SCALP and GOLD SWING price their levels on the **XM bridge** first; GOLD ULTRA, GOLD DIRECTION, GOLD PRO and OPTI GOLD **have no XM leg** and read Binance XAUUSDT, then PAXG; OMNIGOLD's resolver settles **every** open XAUUSD record with whatever bars it fetched, by design (*"a user who runs only one gold tab should still see their records resolve"*), and GOLD SWING's own comment said *"the venue affects execution, not whether the setup resolved"*. **It affects whether the setup resolved:** `macro.js` puts PAXG *"within ~0.5%"* of spot and `goldscalp.js` records XAUT **~1.5%** under the XM print, and in GOLD SCALP's committed walk (2,605 trades) the median stop is **0.219%** of entry and **91.4%** of stops are narrower than 0.5% (99.7% narrower than 1.5%; GOLD SWING's median is 1.02%, 89.5% under 1.5%). A record priced on one feed and settled on another is decided **on its first bar by the basis and the direction of the trade**, not by the tape — a short reads as a win under a feed that prints lower, a long as a loss. Found one seam along from hg-v978: the ledger now knew which bar a record read, and still not whose prices.
- **The rule lives once, beside the timeframe rule it mirrors.** A record carries **`feed`**, the label its own desk's feed chain gave the leg the levels were priced on (`xm-xauusd`, `binance-xau`, `binance-paxg`, `delta-xaut`, …); every resolver names the feed of the bars it hands over (`hgFwdResolve(sym, tf, rows, feed)`, `hgFwdResolveMulti(sym, byTf, feedByTf)` taking one label or the desks' own per-timeframe `gold.src` map); **a KNOWN mismatch is held for its own feed** (`hgFwdFeedFits`), and unknown on either side **fails open** — a record with no feed, or bars whose caller named none, settle exactly as before, because stranding records is the louder failure. A held record is not lost: the desk that priced it re-reads its own feed on its next scan (GOLD SCALP every 3 min, every desk on the 10-min sweep). `hgFwdFeedHeld` counts what waits, and the gold desk note (`hgGoldFwdNote(tab, mechanic, feed)`) prints **N OPEN RECORDS PRICED ON ANOTHER FEED** naming both feeds — rendered even before the desk has a verdict, because a desk with nothing settled can still have records waiting.
- **Thirteen gold files record and nine resolve, each named as its own chain names it:** GOLD SCALP stamps the **15m** leg's label and GOLD SWING the **4h** leg's onto the candidates before publishing (only where absent, so a conviction-locked row keeps the feed it was priced on; the snapshot carries it and SUPER GOLD forwards it across its record map); GOLD ULTRA and GOLD PINE record the feed of **each leg** (`srcByTf`, new on both fetches); GOLD DIRECTION, which mints on more than one timeframe and records on one, names a feed **only when every leg agrees** (`gdFeedUniform`, a mixed fetch names none rather than the wrong one); OMNIGOLD's shared fetcher carries **`feed`** beside `source` (the shell's own per-timeframe record, `null` when it has none — never the `binance-xau` display fallback) and NEW GOLD, 80PERCENT and TAURIC read it off that pack; GOLD PRO names the three places its plan's rows can come from (an exchange perp leg is named as a perp, which no gold-chain resolver reads, so such a record is honest rather than settled on someone else's feed); OMNIGOLD 1 hands the 1h feed it loaded. **The crypto resolvers name no feed and settle as before** (asserted so a later pack knows it was a choice).
- **Driven end to end:** seven XM-priced GOLD SCALP records under PAXG bars 1.5% under the print settle **nothing** and are counted held (the note says so; under the record's own feed it says nothing), settle **7/7 on the tape** under XM bars — and stripped of their feed, the pre-pack record, the same seven under the same PAXG bars resolve **4 of 7 on the FIRST bar, every short a win**. The ledger rule is driven on every shape of input, the defect reproduced first (a feedless long under a flat tape shifted +1% is a target hit on bar one), and the dedup key is proved untouched.
- **Found on the way, two defects:** **(1) TAURIC never priced a plan in production.** `hgOgFetchRows` resolves to a **pack** (`{ rows, source }`) and the price path read `rows.length` on it — `undefined` — so every successful fetch reported *"no gold bars came back"*; its guard stubbed the fetcher with a **bare array**, which is why it passed. Both shapes are read now, and the guard drives the pack. **(2) hg-v978's census read the entry point by NAME** and could not see **GOLD PINE**, which records through a local alias (`var rec = gfn('hgFwdRecordScan')`), so that desk's rows carried neither bar nor feed. Its record builder takes the desk's own fetch now — each lane's feed and each lane's **own signal bar** — and the census here is **derived by call shape**, aliases included, with the call span read to its balanced close so a fourth argument containing parens is counted rather than cut.
- **Five textual guards pinned the old call text** (`test-gold-settle-timeframe`, `test-gold-ledger-dated-on-the-bar`, and — caught by the full suite, not by me — `test-gold-forward-read`, `test-goldscalp-htf-leg`, `test-v694-goldpine-measured-edge`) and went red with the rule untouched, because a resolve, a note or a record builder gained an argument; all five match the rule now. **No gate is loosened, no threshold moves.** Records already written carry no feed and settle as they always did.
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-ledger-same-feed.mjs` (97 assertions; **44/44** behavioural mutations caught across `hg-forward.js`, `gold-forward-read.js`, `goldscalp.js`, `goldswing.js`, `goldultra.js`, `golddirection.js`, `tauric.js`, `optigold.js`, `eightypercent.js`, `omnigold.js`, `omnigold1.js`, `newgold.js` and `goldpine.js`, baseline verified green before every pass).

### Seven gold desks never told the forward ledger which bar they read, so every one of their records was dated on the floor of the scan clock — hg-v978
- **`hg-forward.js` has documented this exact defect since the CRYPTO SCAN fix:** a record dated on the floor of now names a bar that had not closed when the setup was made; settlement walks rows **strictly after** `barT`, so the first bar of the trade — on a 15m scalp, where most fills and stops happen — is skipped; and the dedup key carries `barT`, so two scans that voted on the **same** closed bar in different clock minutes become **two records**. Its remedy was `c.barT`, and **GOLD SCALP, GOLD SWING, GOLD DIRECTION, GOLD ULTRA, GOLD PRO, OPTI GOLD and SUPER GOLD never passed one** — found one field along from hg-v977, whose `signalT` is exactly the instant the ledger had been asking for.
- **The ledger reads `signalT` when no `barT` was given** (ms or seconds, floored to the record timeframe, never in the future; junk falls back to the clock bar exactly as before, so every caller that passed nothing is unchanged). The two home desks and SUPER GOLD **forward it across the record maps that rebuild rows** (the hg-v955 seam, which would otherwise have dropped it a third time). GOLD DIRECTION, GOLD ULTRA, GOLD PRO and OPTI GOLD **say which bar they read** through the bar reader each already has — the 1h signal bar (`gdSignalSec`), the 15m signal bar (`guSignalSec`), the last closed bar of the series in hand (`gpProBarSec`), the break bar of each setup (`ogBreakBarSec`) — the same instant their weekend and news marks are judged on, so the two can never disagree.
- **Driven end to end:** two GOLD SCALP scans on two clock bars record each of **7** levelled candidates **once**, every record dated on the 15m signal bar — it was **fourteen** records dated on two clock bars; two GOLD SWING scans on two 4h clock bars record each of 2 candidates once, dated on the 4h bar. The ledger rule is driven directly on every shape of instant, and **the defect is reproduced first**: the same row with no instant is two records across two clock bars. **The census is derived:** exactly eight gold files record through this entry point (OMNIGOLD, TAURIC and 80PERCENT write by another route and already carry a bar) and none is bare.
- **No gate is loosened, no threshold moves.** What changes is the **date** on the records seven gold desks are judged on — the bar they read — so settlement starts on the first bar of the trade and one firing is one record. Records already written keep their clock-bar dates; nothing is rewritten.
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-ledger-dated-on-the-bar.mjs` (35 assertions; **14/14** behavioural mutations caught across `hg-forward.js`, `goldscalp.js`, `goldswing.js`, `super-gold.js`, `golddirection.js`, `goldultra.js`, `goldpro.js` and `optigold.js`, baseline verified green before every pass).

### The two home gold desks judged their own signal on the wall clock, and SUPER GOLD's per-candidate instant reader had nothing to read — hg-v977
- **hg-v952, v963, v973, v974 and v975 each moved a borrowing desk from `Date.now()` to the signal bar — and GOLD SCALP and GOLD SWING, the desks the mints belong to, went on handing `Date.now()` to their own mint** (the news gate and the weekend mark), the killzone stamp, the ranker's news caution, the watch list, the best-levels session boost, the Part-engine news gates, the A+ cash-open read and the forming stack. On GOLD SWING the last closed 4h bar can sit **four hours** behind the clock, which is exactly what hg-v973 fixed on GOLD PINE's swing lane while the home desk kept the defect.
- **Measured on the shipped tree**, driving each desk headless under a patched clock on the **same bars**: GOLD SCALP read **7 grade-A** candidates in the NY session with the clock 5 min past the bar and **7 with two downgraded to B and every one stamped OFF-SESSION** with it 6 h past; GOLD SWING read both candidates `NY AM` then `OFF-HOURS`; a CPI ten minutes after the bar **locked both desks under the first clock and neither under the second**; a high-impact release that is not tier-1 cautioned (the −2 news part, grade A → B) under the first clock and not the second.
- **Each scan reads one signal-bar instant** (`gsScanBarMs` / `gwScanBarMs`: the last closed 15m bar on SCALP, 4h on SWING, through the one bar reader) for everything that judges the bar, and **keeps the wall clock for what is wall time**: conviction age, the weekend-exposure countdown and demote, the scan stamp, the 7-step feed-staleness read. GOLD SWING **re-reads** its news caution and killzone label on the bar once the bars arrive (`gwNewsCaution` / `gwSessionTxt`, one function each). Driven end to end under two clocks 5 min and 6 h past the bar: **identical boards**, the bar's session, the news lock and the caution on the bar, and the scan stamp still the clock.
- **SUPER GOLD:** `sgCandSec` (hg-v952) has read `c.signalT` on every candidate and **fallen back to the wall clock every time**, because no mint ever wrote that field. Both mints stamp `signalT` = the instant they judged on, both `publishScan` copies carry it across the publish boundary (the hg-v955 seam), and SUPER GOLD hands the best-levels pass the candidate's own instant and the ranker the **newest** signal bar among the candidates it ranks (`sgRankInstant`; the wall clock only when none carries one). The OMNIGOLD engines-panel forming stack reads the 15m bar (`hgOgRowsBarMs`).
- **Two survivors in the first mutation pass were fixture-equivalent** — a tier-1 lock empties the board before the caution can show — and are caught now on a release that cautions without locking; the display-only killzone read paints HTML the snapshot does not carry and is asserted **textually**, and says so (hg-v956).
- **No gate is loosened, no threshold moves.** What changes is **when** the rules on the two home desks are judged: on the bar, like the seven desks that borrow from them.
- **The full suite caught a grep-satisfiable guard, not me:** `tests/test-trading-accuracy.mjs` proved that GOLD SWING's formation batch runs **after** the weekend demotes by searching for the call's exact text, instant name included, so renaming that instant turned it red with the ordering untouched. It matches the call without its last argument now.
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-home-desks-signal-bar.mjs` (39 assertions; **18/18** behavioural mutations caught across `goldscalp.js`, `goldswing.js`, `goldind.js`, `super-gold.js` and `omnigold.js`, baseline verified green before every pass); `tests/test-trading-accuracy.mjs` corrected.

### STAR TRADER's gold lane names its feed and takes the shared live feed; GOLD SWING's feed label is ornamental, and is said to be — hg-v976
- **hg-v974 fed this lane news, macro, the 1h / 4h legs and the 15m signal bar, and stopped at "the desk carries no feed label on this path" and no live feed at all.** Both were reachable. The rows come through `startraderCandles`, whose gold branch walks the XM bridge → `getXAUCandles` → the macro chain → Yahoo, and **every leg knows its source**; and `stWarmContext` is async, so the one shared loader (hg-v971 / v972) can ride on the context the lane already receives. Unfed, the mint **trusted** proxy volume the GOLD SCALP desk distrusts, and the spread lock, the DOM rule and the OI / funding reads were unchecked on the vote this desk casts.
- **The candle chain records the gold source per timeframe** (`startraderGoldSource(tf)`), where the rows are fetched and **only when rows came back**: the XM bridge is the broker feed; `getXAUCandles` reads the shell's per-timeframe record (`S.goldSrcByTf`, the same record the DATA chip reads — never the last-fetched-TF label); the macro chain names its payload; Yahoo is named as `macro.js` names it. A crypto fetch never touches it, a 4h fetch leaves the 15m record alone, a timeframe nothing fetched reads null. The lane hands the **15m** label to the mint and applies `ctx.goldLive` through the **one** applier, which fills only what the input carries none of; `stWarmContext` loads the feed once, null on every failure. **Driven on the real mint through the real lane:** seed 102 casts a GOLD SCALP short vote; a wide **unnamed** quote on the feed **locks** the mint and the vote is gone; the same width naming `delta-xaut`, with the book beside it, keeps the vote (reported, not gated); seed 120 votes unnamed, named `binance-paxg` the volume votes are withheld and **the vote is gone**, the broker label keeps it — parity with GOLD SCALP, which has always named its feed.
- **GOLD SWING's label is ornamental.** The gold-tabs line above said the desk passes `candleSource` "for volume-trust". The swing mint reads that field **nowhere** — the board is byte-identical with and without a PAXG label on every tape — so the line is corrected, the field is **asserted unread** at its two set sites, and it is **not wired**: a swing volume-trust rule would be a new gate on a desk with no measurement of what it removes (the hg-v966 trap), and it would have to be measured on the 244-trade swing walk, which cannot be re-run here (403 CONNECT). The swing replay walk hands no label either, and that changes nothing for the same reason.
- **One vacuous fixture in this pack's own guard, caught by its own mutation pass:** the crypto case named `BTCUSDT`, a contract the catalog does not carry (it keys crypto as `BTCUSD`), so the branch never ran and a mutation recording a gold source there **survived**. Reachability is asserted first now.
- **No gate is loosened, no threshold moves, no new writer of any global** — one module-private record and its reader are added, and every quote this lane can deliver today names the proxy venue, so it is reported and never gated; the volume-trust rule can only remove reads a broker feed never had.
- Tests: `tests/test-startrader-gold-lane-fed.mjs` (37 assertions; **12/12** behavioural mutations caught across `startrader.js` and `startradertab.js`, baseline verified green before every pass).

### The seventh route into the gold mints, the inline GOLD tab's engine bridge, ran unfed — and the census that called hg-v974's two desks the last could not see it — hg-v975
- **`hgGoldInlineBridge` (`goldind.js`) calls `goldScalpSetups` and `goldSwingSetups` directly, and the inline `runGold()` in `index.html` handed it bars, macro and `Date.now()`:** no `news` under the key the mint reads (the hg-v963 literal in an **eighth** place), no feed label (so the mint **trusted** proxy volume the GOLD SCALP desk distrusts), no live quote or book (spread lock and DOM rule unchecked), and the wall clock for both lanes. The scalp leader it printed on the stat line was one the fed mint might have locked; the swing result it computed was **never printed at all** (hg-v932). hg-v974's "last two borrowers" rested on a census that was a grep for `gfn()` lookups, which cannot see a direct call inside `goldind.js` itself.
- **The bridge is fed like the other six routes now:** the news snapshot under the key the mint reads, the **15m** feed label (volume trust), the shared live feed through the **one** applier (hg-v971 / v972: only where the input carries nothing), and each lane judged on its **own signal bar** — 15m for the scalp mint, 4h for the swing mint — with a caller's wall clock demoted to a fallback for a series with no readable instant. **Driven for real through the exported bridge on the real mints:** a CPI ten minutes after the last 15m bar locks both lanes (seed 102: **7 → 0** scalp, **2 → 0** swing) *with the caller passing the wall clock*, where the mint handed that clock mints on — so it is the **bridge** that chose the bar; a release three hours out mints again; a wide **unnamed** quote on the live feed locks both lanes and the same width naming `delta-xaut` moves **no** board; a broker quote already on the input **wins** over the feed; named `binance-paxg` empties the seed-120 board (**2 → 0**) while the broker label keeps it; a release ten minutes after the **4h** bar locks the swing lane alone (**1 → 0**) and leaves the scalp lane, whose bar is three hours later, untouched — two instants, one per lane. The `runGold` block is **lifted out of `index.html` and run** under stubs: `news`, the 15m label over the last-fetched TF, the live feed awaited, the macro, no wall clock; a rejecting feed, an absent feed or an absent snapshot **fail open**.
- **The stat line says what happened:** a lane that formed candidates and can crown none reads `N formed, none can lead` (hg-v940) instead of exactly like a lane that formed nothing; the swing lane is printed (`hgInlineGoldBridgeLine`); a null tally prints **no** tally, because `isFinite(null)` is `true` and the old line would have printed `tally +null` — a latent **eleventh** instance of that coercion.
- **The census is derived, not typed (hg-v954):** the guard finds every file that calls either mint by its **call shape** (direct call, property read, lookup), comments stripped, and requires the set to agree with the declared routes **in both directions** — eleven files today: the two definitions, the six desk routes, the three replay harnesses — so a new borrower must declare itself or be named. The ranker's own rows were **not** added to the bridge's ranking context: measured on the real scalp mint, the confluence verdict is stamped inside the mint before the ranker runs, so rows there change nothing (the hg-v700 starvation was the swing **route**, fixed in hg-v973), and an input that changes nothing is the ornamental field.
- **No gate is loosened, no threshold moves, no new writer of any global.** What changes is that the seventh route **asks** the rules the mints carry instead of answering for them; every quote this route can deliver today names the proxy venue and is reported, never gated, and the volume-trust rule can only remove reads a broker feed never had.
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-inline-bridge-fed.mjs` (40 assertions; **17/17** behavioural mutations caught across `goldind.js` and `index.html`, baseline verified green before every pass).

### The last two borrowers of the gold scalp mint, OMNIGOLD's engine bridge and STAR TRADER's gold lane, fed at last — hg-v974
- **Every other desk that borrows `goldScalpSetups` / `goldSwingSetups` was fed over hg-v971…v973. Two callers were left.** (1) **OMNIGOLD's bridge** (`hgOgRunGoldTabEngines`) copied rows, macro and news into the mint input and **nothing else** — while `shared`, built by the same scan, has carried the desk's **quote** (bid / ask / spread / venue from the Delta payload, hg-v969), its **perp payload** and the **feed label** since those packs shipped. So the two borrowed mints ran on OMNIGOLD without the spread lock, the L2 book, the OI / funding reads or the volume-trust rule, and on the wall clock. (2) **STAR TRADER** handed the mint `newsState` — a key the mint has **never read** (its key is `news`), so the news gate failed open there on every scan — and 15m bars alone: no 1h / 4h legs, no macro, the wall clock; and its vote read `top.kind`, a field no candidate carries.
- **The bridge builds the applier feed from `shared`** (`hgOgBridgeFeedFromShared`: the hg-v969 quote fields, a broker quote on `shared` winning over them, the book read off the payload through the **one** reader, the payload itself), applies it through the **one applier** every borrowing desk uses (hg-v971), names the scan's own feed label (both call sites pass `res.scalp.source`), and judges **each lane on its own signal bar**. **Driven for real through the exported bridge:** a wide unnamed quote locks both lanes; a wide proxy quote moves no board and lands `SPREAD WIDE` on every card of both lanes; the book reports `L2 READ`; named `binance-paxg` takes every scalp candidate **one agreeing read down** while the broker label keeps them and **no label invents nothing**; a CPI ten minutes after the last 15m bar locks both lanes where the wall clock (months away) could not; a release ten minutes after the 4h bar locks the **swing lane alone** — two instants, one per lane.
- **STAR TRADER hands the mint input shape the other desks use**: `news` under the key the mint reads, the 1h and 4h legs, the desk's own warmed `getGoldMacro` read (the cached snapshot when unwarmed, null with neither), the 15m signal bar, and a vote that names the strategy. **The real mint through the real lane proves it:** seed 102 casts a GOLD SCALP short vote, and a CPI ten minutes after the signal bar removes it — the gate that never fired here. The desk carries no feed label on this path, so volume trust there stays as it was: **reported, not invented**.
- **The trap this pack's own guard caught:** the first cut of the feed builder tested `isFinite(+shared.spreadUsd)`, and `+null` is `0`, so a **null spread became a zero-width quote** — the **tenth** instance of that coercion in this codebase, inside a pack written after nine. A missing value is `NaN` there now, never zero. **And a mutation defaulting the feed label to the proxy survived** a board comparison alone, because the board omits the agreeing-read count; the check is read for read now.
- **Not done, and why:** the bridge ranks the mint output (`goldRankSetups` re-orders and re-grades from its own tally), so the guard's observable through it is the agreeing-read count per mechanic, not the grade. The daily bars already reached the MTF matrix through `rows1d`; an alias under the scalp mint's own key was tried, proved equivalent, and dropped rather than shipped ornamental.
- **No gate is loosened, no threshold moves.** What changes is that two more desks **ask** the rules the mints carry instead of answering for them; on OMNIGOLD every quote and book delivered names the proxy venue, so it is reported and never gated, and the volume-trust rule can only remove reads a broker feed never had.
- **The full suite caught a stale claim in the hg-v970 guard, not me:** that guard asserted OMNIGOLD **and** OMNIGOLD 1 *do not take the book: they have no reader that can decide on it*, over the **whole** of `omnigold.js`. True of OMNIGOLD's native path, where the book sits below a quote that always arrives; **no longer true of the file**, because the bridge now reads the book off the payload for the borrowed mints, which carry the DOM rule hg-v970 itself made venue-aware, and `L2 READ` is proved landing through it. The assertion is **bounded** now: the reader is required inside the bridge feed builder, forbidden everywhere else in the file, and still absent from OMNIGOLD 1. Nothing about what hg-v970 refused to wire has changed.
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-last-borrowers-fed.mjs` (32 assertions; **20/20** behavioural mutations caught across `omnigold.js` and `startradertab.js`, baseline verified green before every pass); `tests/test-gold-l2-book-fed.mjs` §7 corrected (**3/3** on the amended bound).

### GOLD PINE's swing lane took a third route into the swing mint that nothing fed — hg-v973
- **hg-v971 fed the three borrowing desks through one applier and listed GOLD PINE as fed. It fed GOLD PINE's SCALP lane.** The SWING lane reaches the mint through `goldswingCollectCandidates`, which called `buildCandidates` with **no `microOpts`** — so the news gate read `news: null` and failed open (the hg-v963 literal in a **seventh** place, on a desk hg-v965 listed covered), the quote, book, venue and cost override of hg-v968…v971 never arrived, the **raw news snapshot** was passed where a caution object was expected, and it ranked with **no rows for the confluence scorer** — so the `CONF NO TRADE` demote this desk applies read `CONF UNCHECKED` there and could not fire (the hg-v700 starvation, one route over). A third route beside `goldSwingSetups` and `runScan`, and the one nothing fed.
- **The route delegates now: one route, one home.** Proved on the real mint: the route returns the **same board as `goldSwingSetups`** stamp for stamp, `CONF NO TRADE` lands where `CONF UNCHECKED` used to read, the **news gate locks through it** (10 min before CPI mints nothing, 3h after mints again), a wide **unnamed** quote locks and a wide **proxy** quote reports (`SPREAD WIDE`), the **L2 book** reports, the **daily bars** reach it (a weekly-range break forms through it only when they do), and the **hg-v953 weekend mark** lands.
- **Both lanes are lifted out of the file and RUN.** The swing lane hands the route the desk's own news and macro, the shared live feed where the input carries nothing, and the **4h signal bar** rather than the wall clock (hg-v952 / hg-v963). The scalp lane fetched **daily bars and never passed them** (the MTF matrix's Daily leg was unchecked on this desk — fed, it stamps `MTF BIAS` on the seed-106 long), **never named its feed**, and judged on the wall clock; it hands `dailyCandles`, `candleSource` and the **15m signal bar** now.
- **The feed label does real work, said plainly.** The mint distrusts PAXG / XAUT volume **only when told which feed it is on** (`volTrusted`), so GOLD PINE, GOLD ULTRA and GOLD DIRECTION — which read PAXG and XAUT bars and never said so — let **proxy volume vote**. Measured on the real scalp mint: named `binance-paxg` withholds the squeeze and MFI votes, **every candidate loses one agreeing read**, two fall from **grade A to B** on one tape, and on another **two candidates leave the board**; an unnamed feed reads exactly like the broker feed. That is **parity with GOLD SCALP**, which has always named its feed, and the rule, its pattern and the two volume reads are unchanged. All three desks name the feed now.
- **The adapter dropped every verdict the mint wrote.** `setupFromNative` kept levels, grade and tally, so a `CONF NO TRADE` / `EDGE DEMOTE` / `MTF BIAS` demote, or a `SPREAD WIDE` / `L2 READ` advisory, never reached a GOLD PINE card (hg-v955). They are carried as a **mark** and **read**: a `MINT DEMOTED · <why>` chip on the card header and the notes printed once under the factors. **The leader rule does not read them** — `gpTapeAligned`, `sortSetups` and `probScore` are asserted untouched, and a mutation making the leader skip demoted rows is **caught**, because that is a policy this pack does not set; this tab's own solidity, tape and handoff-floor gates still decide.
- **Two grep-satisfiable assertions in this pack's own guard, caught by its own mutation pass:** matching the chip and notes readers by name was satisfied by their **definitions** while the card called neither. Bounded to the call sites inside `cardHTML` now. **Two earlier guards** (hg-v971, hg-v972) lifted the scalp lane without the new signal-bar helper and went red; they lift the real helper instead of stubbing it. **Two proposed mutations** sat on seams outside this pack (the hg-v700 scorer rows in `goldSwingSetups`, and a second consumer of the daily bars) and are dropped, not counted.
- **This pack can remove candidates from three boards** (the volume-trust rule) and **can lock GOLD PINE's swing lane** (the news gate, the spread lock) — each the pre-existing rule of the mint those desks borrow, reached at last. No gate is loosened, no threshold moves, no new writer of any global is added.
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-pine-lanes-fed.mjs` (69 assertions; **30/30** behavioural mutations caught across `goldswing.js`, `goldpine.js`, `goldind.js`, `golddirection.js` and `goldultra.js`, baseline verified green before every pass).

### The macro snapshot never reached the three desks that borrow the gold mints — hg-v972
- **hg-v971 gave GOLD PINE, GOLD ULTRA and GOLD DIRECTION one shared live feed for the borrowed GOLD SCALP / GOLD SWING mints, and it carried the quote, the book and the perp payload — NOT the macro snapshot.** Those three desks handed the mints no `macro` at all (GOLD PINE fetched one for its own scoring and **dropped it at the mint seam**), so **`hgGoldMacroLock`** — the 20-day DXY+TNX gold-long kill, a **hard drop on both filter paths** — and the forming regime's dollar / real-yield reads ran **unchecked** on those desks on every scan, while the two desks the mints belong to have applied that lock since hg-v553. Found by tracing which inputs each lane hands the mint, one field along from where hg-v971 stopped.
- **The feed now carries it.** `hgGoldLiveFeed` reads the **same `getGoldMacro`** every fed desk reads, under the same timer, falling back to the last cached snapshot (`getGoldMacroCached`) on a timeout — the band reads 20-day trends that do not move inside a scan — and to null on every failure, never a snapshot invented. `hgGoldApplyLiveFeed` fills `inp.macro` **only where the input carries none**, so GOLD PINE's own fetch, now carried into the mint instead of dropped, wins over the shared feed. Since the hg-v971 lanes already call the applier, all three desks get it with no new lane edits; each lane is lifted and **run** against a recording mint to prove the snapshot arrives.
- **This pack CAN remove longs from those three boards, and says so.** Proved on the real mints: DXY RISING + TNX RISING drops every gold **long** with `CONVICTION LOCK` through the scalp mint, the swing mint and the applier seam the three desks use, and leaves every **short** identical; a **FLAT** snapshot or **one** bullish leg moves **no** board. That is **parity** with the mints those desks borrow, not a new policy — the rule, its bar and its 20-day band (`hgGoldMacroRule` still `trend20`) are untouched.
- **What is deliberately NOT fed, and why:** the **5-bar yield guard** (`validateYieldCorrelation`, demote on scalp, DROP on swing) reads `inp.us10yCandles`, and **`macro.js` has never written that field** — so it has been unfed on **every** gold desk since it shipped, the two fed desks included, which copy it from a snapshot that never carries it. It is a **different rule** from the 20-day band, unmeasured, and feeding it because the plumbing is at hand is the hg-v966 trap exactly. The guard proves it is **live** on the top-level field (a rising 10-year stamps `MACRO YIELD`), **unreachable** through the snapshot, **unsupplied** by `macro.js` (the real `getGoldMacro` is driven and its keys read), and that the applier writes `inp.macro` and **no sub-field**. Reported, not fed.
- **Two equivalent mutants in the first cut, both the same shape:** the macro leg carried its own shape test and its own catch **beside** the ones on the raced promise, and removing either changed nothing — a duplicated check is an unkillable mutant. The duplicates are gone and the single home is proved by the pass.
- **No gate is loosened, no threshold moves, no new writer of any global is added.** What changes is that three desks now **ask** the macro lock instead of answering for it.
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-live-feed-macro.mjs` (55 assertions; **28/28** behavioural mutations caught across `goldind.js`, `goldpine.js`, `goldswing.js`, `golddirection.js` and `goldultra.js`, baseline verified green before every pass).

### The quote hg-v968 fed never reached the two mints it was fed to, and three more desks borrow those mints unfed — hg-v971
- **Two defects in my own packs, found by tracing the seam rather than trusting the guard that passed.** (1) On GOLD SCALP and GOLD SWING the quote the hg-v968 perp handler put on `ctx` was **never copied into the bundle the mint reads** (`scalpBundle` / `microOpts` read only the zero-writer globals), so the spread lock had **still never received a quote** on either desk — fed to a seam that dropped it, hg-v932 in my own pack for the third time. hg-v968's guard lifted the handler and asserted `ctx`; hg-v969's did the same; neither drove the seam after it. (2) `goldScalpSetups` and `goldSwingSetups` build the institutional-filter context **without the venue**, so a quote that *had* arrived would have **LOCKED** rather than been reported — the hg-v968 proxy rule was unreachable through the mints. Only OMNIGOLD (hg-v969) carried the venue through. Both are proved on the **real mints**: a wide quote naming `delta-xaut` now moves **no board** and lands a `SPREAD WIDE` note on every card, while the same quote **unnamed** or naming the broker locks every candidate — which is what a live proxy quote would have done the moment (1) was fixed without (2).
- **Three desks borrow those mints and fetched no payload at all:** GOLD PINE, GOLD ULTRA and GOLD DIRECTION call `goldScalpSetups` / `goldSwingSetups` with bars only, so the spread lock, the DOM rule and the OI / funding reads were `unchecked` there on every scan. Each now loads the feed **once per scan, awaited, before its lanes**, and each lane hands it to the mint — proved by lifting each lane's real function out of the file and **running** it against a recording mint, with and without a feed.
- **One loader, one applier, five desks (hg-v949):** `hgGoldLiveFeed` loads the payload through the existing `hgGoldLoadDeltaPerp` and turns it into `{ perp, quote, l2 }` through the two existing readers — no third parser — with a **timeout** so no desk waits forever on a quote, and nulls on every failure. `hgGoldApplyLiveFeed` puts it on a mint input **only where the input carries nothing**, so a named broker quote or book on the global still wins; that property is proved on the applier directly **and** by lifting and running each fed desk's real bundle-build block with a broker quote on the global. The two hg-v970 seam lines fold into the applier and that guard's textual assertions move with them.
- **A grep-satisfiable assertion in this pack's own guard, caught by its own mutation pass:** the first cut matched the applier call in the source, and an `if (false)` in front of it **survived** on both desks. The bundle block is now lifted and executed instead.
- **Nothing leaves any board.** Every quote and book this pack delivers names the proxy venue, so on all five desks it is reported and never gated; the `$0.25` bar is unchanged and asserted unchanged; no writer of the globals is added.
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-live-feed-reaches-mints.mjs` (66 assertions; **23/23** behavioural mutations caught across `goldind.js`, `goldscalp.js`, `goldswing.js`, `golddirection.js`, `goldultra.js` and `goldpine.js`, baseline verified green before every pass).

### The gold L2 book is fed, and the DOM rule it feeds is made venue-aware before it can empty a board — hg-v970
- **hg-v968 proved three quote globals had zero writers and fed the bid/ask one; the L2 book (`__hgGoldL2Book`) still had none**, so the gold DOM rule — `validateDomLiquidity`, reached from GOLD SCALP's and GOLD SWING's micro veto — **had never run on any desk**. The book rides the same Delta payload the quote does: `/v2/l2orderbook/{symbol}` is a sixth leg of `fetchDeltaPerpHistory`, in **both** parser copies, driven on both with an injected fetch (the server uses the `.cjs`, hg-v968). One reader, `hgGoldL2FromPerp`, beside `hgGoldQuoteFromPerp`; the desks do not parse the payload themselves (asserted).
- **The trap, found before the wire went in, IS the pack.** That rule **vetoes unless the book skews ≥20% toward the trade** — a **balanced** book vetoes both sides — and on GOLD SCALP a non-demote micro veto is a **DROP**. Feeding a thin gold-proxy book to a rule that strict, unmeasured, would have emptied every gold board: the hg-v966 trap exactly, a feed handed to a rule nobody decided, and hg-v919's asymmetry (a broker-book rule read against a crypto proxy). **Driven on the real mints:** the same balanced book with **no venue** drops the scalp board and the swing board to **zero** — the pre-existing rule, untouched — while the same book naming **`delta-xaut`** removes **nothing**, leaves entry / stop / T1 / direction / demotion **identical**, and lands an **L2 READ** note once on every card.
- **So the spread lock's venue rule (hg-v968) applies here too, and it is ONE rule:** `hgGoldSpreadVenueOk` is the basis-venue test for both. A book naming a non-broker venue is **reported, never gated** (`triggerValid:true, advisory:true`, naming the venue, the skew and its size, and that it is not gated); a book naming **no** venue, or the broker, behaves **exactly as before** (the original `L2 VETO` reasons, byte for byte). A named broker book on the global still **wins** — the proxy fills the seam only when nothing else has.
- **The advisory is READ, not written and forgotten** (hg-v955 / hg-v969): both micro vetoes return `{ advisory, note }` with **no `reason` key**, because a `reason` is a veto to every caller; the scalp caller pushes it onto `c.notes`, the swing caller carries it onto the candidate's `notes`, and `evaluateScalp` / `evaluateSwing` record `domNote` without touching validity.
- **What is deliberately NOT wired, and why:** OMNIGOLD passes the book only into the spread lock's L2 fallback, *below* the quote that always arrives on the same payload, so the book could never decide there; OMNIGOLD 1 passes `inp.dom` into a context nothing reads. Wiring either is the ornamental field. Both are asserted un-wired rather than left to memory.
- **A vacuous fixture in this pack's own guard, caught before shipping:** on the hg-v953 swing seed the two candidates were Part8/Part9 **direct** mints, which never reach `mkCand`'s micro veto, so every swing assertion passed or failed vacuously — a seed search found one (103, falling tape) that mints `bos` + `p6comp` through the path that carries the rule, and the guard now asserts that path was reached **first**.
- **One survivor is an EQUIVALENT MUTANT and is reported as one:** relaxing the reader's both-sides guard leaves the best-level check and the `try` returning null on the same inputs — proved identical on **34** inputs.
- **No gate is loosened, no threshold moves, no new writer of the globals is added, and nothing leaves any board** — the only verdict this rule can now reach is the one it always could, on a broker book that still does not exist.
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-l2-book-fed.mjs` (102 assertions; **28/29** behavioural mutations caught across `goldind.js`, `goldscalp.js`, `goldswing.js` and both `delta-perp-history` copies, the twenty-ninth proved equivalent, baseline verified green before every pass).

### The spread lock was fed on two of the four desks that read it, and the advisory it wrote was read by nothing — hg-v969
- **hg-v968 fed the gold spread lock from the Delta perp payload on GOLD SCALP and GOLD SWING, and stopped there.** OMNIGOLD fetches the **same** payload on every scan (`hgGoldLoadDeltaPerp`, since hg-v554) and its institutional filter is a **HARD ledger row** (hg-v551) — and it went on reading bid / ask from `__hgGoldQuote` alone, the global hg-v968 itself proved has **zero writers**. OMNIGOLD 1 awaits the same payload and did the same, so its DATA LOAD line read **no quote** on every scan since the tab was built. Two desks fed, two documented harder and left dead: the hg-v967 trap one desk along.
- **And the advisory verdict hg-v968 wrote was ORNAMENTAL.** It wrote `cand.spreadLock.advisory` so a proxy-venue spread would be *reported, never dropped* — and **nothing read the field**. A report nobody can see is the hg-v955 finding, in my own previous pack. It is read now: GOLD SCALP and GOLD SWING both paint `c.notes`, so the note lands there **once** (a filter run twice does not print it twice); OMNIGOLD folds it into the **inst-filter row's own `why`**, so the ledger says `SPREAD WIDE — … on delta-xaut, a gold proxy … reported, not gated` beside a **PASS**, and a broker-venue quote of the same width still reads **VETO · SPREAD LOCK** — the `$0.25` bar is unchanged and asserted unchanged.
- **One reader, still.** All four desks call `hgGoldQuoteFromPerp`; the guard asserts no desk parses `best_bid` / `best_ask` itself (hg-v949). **A named broker quote on the global still wins** — the proxy fills the seam only when nothing else has — and a half quote, a missing payload or an absent `goldind.js` leave the context exactly as before, proved by lifting each desk's **real** handler out of the file and running it (hg-v951) rather than stubbing the reader.
- **Nothing leaves any board.** OMNIGOLD's feed chain ends in Delta XAUTUSD, a gold **proxy**, so every quote this pack supplies there names a non-broker venue and is **reported, never dropped** (hg-v919's asymmetry, hg-v968's rule). On OMNIGOLD 1 the spread veto (`live spread > 2× 20d hour average`) needs a 20-day average **nothing supplies**, so it stays **unchecked** with or without the quote — the guard runs the engine both ways and requires the veto state, the clear flag and the matrix score to be **identical**. What changes is a load line that now reads `partial · measured on delta-xaut (gold proxy, not the broker book)` instead of `no quote`.
- **Two seams are asserted textually, and say so** (hg-v956): the shared-context → per-horizon `extra` → `runEval` hand-off lives inside the scan closure and is not lifted; whether the venue survives it is a property of the source.
- **No gate is loosened, no threshold moves, no new writer of the quote globals is added** (the hg-v968 zero-writers partition is re-asserted on a non-empty sweep).
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-spread-lock-omnigold.mjs` (62 assertions; **16/16** behavioural mutations caught across `goldind.js`, `omnigold.js` and `omnigold1.js`, baseline verified green before every pass).

### The 3-minute GOLD SCALP refresh was INVISIBLE, and the spread lock had never fired — hg-v968
- **Asked three times for an auto-updating GOLD SCALP tab.** hg-v965 put the desk on a 3-minute clock, hg-v967 fixed the resolution that stopped it firing — and **this tab showed no last-scan time anywhere**, so a working refresh and a dead one looked *identical*. Two answers changed a timer the desk owner had no way to observe. **The mechanism was delivered and the observable outcome was not**, which is the actual reason it kept coming back.
- **The tab now carries a live line** — `AUTO 3m · last scan HH:MM:SS · next in M:SS`. It is stamped in the `runScan` **finally**, so a **failed** scan stamps too (a frozen clock is not information), and the countdown is **read from the shell timer** rather than recomputed, so it cannot drift from the clock it describes; with the shell absent it **omits** the countdown instead of inventing one. One page, one ticker (hg-v958).
- **Separately, the gold SPREAD LOCK had never fired either.** This file documents it as a live hard gate — *live bid/ask wider than 250 points / 2.5 pips ($0.25) kills the entry* — and the globals it reads (`__hgGoldQuote`, `__hgGoldSpreadUsd`, `__hgGoldL2Book`) have **37 read sites across four desks and ZERO writers** anywhere in the repo, so it returned `unchecked` on every scan since it shipped. The guard **derives** that partition rather than hard-coding it.
- **The quote was already on the wire.** Delta's `/v2/tickers/{symbol}` returns a `quotes` object, and `parseDeltaTicker` reached into it for `mark_price` while **dropping `best_bid` / `best_ask`** — the exact two fields the lock reads. hg-v932's *work done and dropped on the way out*, in the gold quote path.
- **Both parser copies are fixed, and the guard drives BOTH** — the server uses the **`.cjs`** while the test imports the **`.mjs`**, so patching only the tested copy would have left production still dropping the quote. That is the hg-v967 trap one file along, and the parity assertion exists to stop it.
- **The bar is venue-specific, and that is handled rather than ignored.** `$0.25` is a **broker** figure; the desks' feed chain ends in **Delta XAUTUSD** and **Binance PAXG**, crypto-settled gold **proxies**. hg-v919 measured that exact asymmetry on the cost ceiling — **96.1%** of the scalp book vetoed at PAXG against **16.4%** at XM — so a quote naming a non-broker venue is **reported and never dropped**. **Nothing leaves any board that was not already leaving it**, because today the lock drops nothing at all; a quote naming **no** venue behaves exactly as before, which is what every existing caller and guard sees.
- **And a third, which HUNG THE WHOLE SUITE:** the countdown ticker was armed at **module load**. Harmless in a browser; in Node it installs a real 1-second `setInterval` that is never cleared, so the event loop never drains and **the process never exits** — the full run stalled on `test-conviction-orphan-expiry` for over three minutes with no output. It is armed from the **mount** now (also the correct lifecycle: a countdown for a tab nobody opened has nothing to paint), and the guard asserts that **loading the file installs no timer at all**. **A fourth, in the assertion written for that:** it read a fixed 4,000 characters after the function name to find the arm site and missed it — the fixed-width-slice brittleness corrected five times before — so it **mounts the real tab** and watches the timer appear instead.
- **Two defects in this pack's own guard:** the repo sweep regex **over-escaped and matched nothing**, so the zero-writers assertion passed **vacuously** on an empty sweep — caught only because the reads-first assertion demanded a non-zero count; and the **half-quote** case was driven on one parser copy while the two were compared only on a **full** quote, leaving a `.mjs`-only fabricated-spread mutation alive.
- **And a fifth, which the full suite caught after the hang fix — the hg-v967 trap, two lines below the comment warning against it:** exporting the two stamp helpers took `goldscalp.js` from **19 to 21** module-scope functions and turned `tests/test-gold-render-integrity.mjs` red, the guard that fuzzes every export *because* this file keeps a small surface. The guard is not relaxed; both helpers travel on the **`HG_tabs` registration**, the single route hg-v967 established, and the guard asserts the globals are **absent**.
- **No gate is loosened and no threshold moves** — the `$0.25` bar is unchanged, and asserted unchanged.
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-spread-lock-fed.mjs` (76 assertions; **30/30** behavioural mutations caught across `goldscalp.js`, `goldswing.js`, `goldind.js` and both `delta-perp-history` copies, baseline verified green before every pass).

### The 3-minute GOLD SCALP refresh never ran once — hg-v967
- **hg-v965 added a dedicated 3-minute clock that calls `window.goldscalpRefresh`, and `goldscalp.js` never put that function on `window`.** It existed only as the `.refresh` property of its `HG_tabs` entry. So every tick resolved `undefined`, returned `'unavailable'`, and the feature asked for **by name** did nothing at all — on every page load, for the whole life of hg-v965 and hg-v966.
- **The guard missed it for a reason worth naming:** it **stubbed** `ctx.goldscalpRefresh` and then asserted the tick called it. That tests the **tick** and says nothing about whether anything in the shipped tree **provides** the function — the grep-satisfiable shape one layer out, and the same class hg-v963 and hg-v964 each caught once. **A stub can only prove the caller; it can never prove the wiring the caller depends on.**
- **The obvious fix is exporting it, and that is REFUSED.** `goldscalpRefresh` **starts a network scan**, so putting it on the global object makes it reachable by anything — and `tests/test-gold-render-integrity.mjs` fuzzes every module-scope export of the gold tabs *precisely because* this file keeps a small surface. Exporting it weakened a guard to fix a caller, and **the full suite said so**: it tipped `goldscalp` from 19 module-scope exports to 20 and turned that test red. The export is **deliberately absent**, the file says why, and the guard **asserts the absence** so a later pack does not helpfully add it back.
- **The fix RESOLVES rather than assumes:** `hgGoldScalpRefreshFn()` prefers a global where one exists and otherwise resolves the **`HG_tabs` entry**, which is the registration the shell's own sweep already uses and therefore the authority here. With neither present the tick reports `'unavailable'` honestly rather than fabricating one.
- **What the new assertions do, since the old ones passed while the feature was dead:** the **real** `goldscalp.js` is booted and the resolver required to find a refresh in it; the registration is asserted directly, so renaming its id or dropping its refresh is caught; the global branch is exercised separately so both routes stay live; a **decoy** GOLD SWING tab is pushed so a resolver matching merely the first tab with any refresh is caught; and the tick is driven with the global **removed** and only the registration present — the exact shape hg-v965 shipped into — so a revert to reading the bare global is caught.
- **Found by driving the real module, not by reading it:** the probe booted `goldscalp.js`, asked for `window.goldscalpRefresh`, and it was not there.
- **No gate is loosened, no threshold moves, no setup leaves any board**, and the scan this clock runs is the desk's own unchanged scan.
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-news-raw-minters.mjs` (118 assertions; **12/12** behavioural mutations caught across `goldscalp.js` and `index.html`, baseline verified green before every pass).

### The gold macro kill ran a different rule than the one it documented — hg-v966
- **`hgGoldMacroLock` is the gold-native kill** — the dollar and the 10-year both bullish stands a gold **LONG** down — and it offers **two** reads of "bullish": **`hgGoldEma50Above`**, a **LEVEL** test of last close against its own EMA50, and a **20-day CHANGE band** (DXY > **0.3%**, TNX > **2%**). Both call sites of the first sit behind `ctx.dxyRows` / `ctx.tnxRows`, and **`getGoldMacro()` — the only macro supplier on every gold desk — returned NEITHER field.** So the documented read was **unreachable**, the band was silently the rule in force, and `goldind.js`, this file and the card all named the wrong one.
- **They are not a strict and a loose version of one rule — they disagree in BOTH directions.** A market well above its EMA50 but **flat** over twenty days is bullish to the first and FLAT to the second; one **below** its EMA50 while rising over twenty days is the reverse. Both directions are proved behaviourally on constructed series, and the **frequency on real markets is NOT claimed** — 403 CONNECT on every route, and no DXY or TNX series is committed.
- **The naive fix is a trap, and that is the finding.** Simply supplying the feed would have handed the verdict to EMA50 on **every** gold desk, because the old chain preferred rows wherever they existed — switching the gold-long kill from a change band to a level test with **nobody deciding that**, and a market is above its EMA50 far more often than it is up 0.3% over twenty days. On the guard's own grid the naive fix moves **3,472** verdicts.
- **So the feed is supplied AND the rule in force decides alone**, proved differentially against the replaced function over **5,425 cases — 250 locks before, 250 after, ZERO verdict differences.** The level test is computed and its **disagreement reported on every scan**, so a machine that can fetch has the measurement this environment cannot make.
- **Why the band and not the level test:** every forward record, every walk and every edge verdict on these desks was generated with the **band** deciding, so switching would put today's board out of step with all the recorded evidence those desks are judged on. That is a reason rather than a preference, and **`HG_GOLD_MACRO_RULE`** is the one lever that reverses it (a junk value is ignored, not obeyed).
- **A fourth defect that would have kept the read dead anyway:** `__rowOk` demands finite `o/h/l/c` and drops every row without them — correct for a candle, **wrong for a macro index**. A DXY computed from FX rates per date has a close and nothing else, so `hgGoldEma50Above` would have returned **null on a perfectly good 180-point series** and this pack would have claimed a live read that was still dead. Found by driving the real function on a real close-only series rather than assuming the supply fix was enough. **The strict candle check is NOT loosened** — the close-only path is a separate normaliser, for callers that read closes exclusively.
- **And a fifth:** `cand.macroLock` was **written and read by nothing** — the ornamental field hg-v955 found in the forward ledger, here in the gold mint — so nothing could report which read decided. **`hgGoldMacroLockNote`** is the reader; it renders **nothing** without a verdict, names the read that decided, and when the two rules disagree says so **and refuses to say which is better**.
- **Two shipped tests changed expectation deliberately**, with the reason recorded in both files rather than in a changelog: `tests/test-gold-inst-gates.mjs` and `tests/test-omnigold-inst-gates.mjs` asserted that supplying the series locks by EMA50 — the file's **intent**, which was never the **production reality**. Both now assert **both branches**, so neither claim is lost.
- **Three defects in this pack's own work**, all caught by driving the real code: the first cut let a **FLAT band fall through to EMA50**, which would have started locking gold longs the desk does not lock today — exactly the unmeasured change this pack refuses; the disagreement was computed **after** the `unchecked` return, throwing the measurement away in the one case where the two rules are furthest apart; and the note rendered a claim about reads never made, because an object carrying no verdict has **undefined** fields rather than null.
- **The `isFinite(null)` trap again** — the eighth time in this codebase and the **second inside a guard written to catch it**: `isFinite(null)` is `true` because `+null === 0`, so a fabricated null close passed a finiteness assertion until it read `Number.isFinite`.
- **No gate is loosened, no threshold moves, and no setup leaves any board.**
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-macro-lock-rule.mjs` (58 assertions; **25/25** behavioural mutations caught across `goldind.js` and `macro.js`, baseline verified green before every pass).

### The last four gold desks get the news gate, and GOLD SCALP gets a 3-minute clock — hg-v965
- **hg-v963 built a coverage reporter so gaps would name themselves, hg-v964 took it from seven uncovered desks to four, and this takes it to ZERO.** OPTI GOLD, 80PERCENT, GOLD (inline) and TAURIC now each have a route, and the `uncovered` bucket is empty for the first time since the reporter existed.
- **No new policy is invented per desk. Each treats the news lock EXACTLY as it already treats the weekend**, because the two calendars make the same claim about the same instant — that new gold minting is barred — and inventing a second, different answer per desk is how four desks end up with four policies nobody can state. **OPTI GOLD** withholds the **ACTIONABLE** claim and keeps ENTRY / STOP / T1 (hg-v950). The **inline GOLD** lanes withhold **both handoffs** (hg-v951). **80PERCENT** and **TAURIC** **mark and withhold nothing** (hg-v950 / hg-v952) — their rows record `ticket:false` and `gateClear:false`, so there is no ticket there to withhold.
- **ZERO new wrappers, and that is the point of the shape.** `optigold.js`, `eightypercent.js` and `tauric.js` carry a **byte-identical** weekend wrapper each; three matching news wrappers beside them would be **six copies of one rule**, which hg-v949 calls a second rule rather than a second copy. So the *null-unless-it-locks* rule lives **once**, as **`hgGoldNewsMark`** in `gold-formation.js`, delegating to hg-v964's `hgGoldNewsVerdict` and adding no calendar, no threshold and no seam of its own. Every call site is a **typeof guard and nothing else**, so there is no rule in them to drift.
- **Every desk reads both calendars on the SAME instant expression**, which is the drift hg-v952 and hg-v963 each paid for once. The inline GOLD lanes go further and read the bar **once** (`hgInlineGoldBarMs`), per lane, because the 4h swing and 15m scalp lanes genuinely judge different moments and one shared instant would mislabel one of them (hg-v950). TAURIC's bar was computed inline inside its record writer and is now **one function** (`hgTauricBarT`) serving the record and the card.
- **The two withhold notices are separate functions on purpose:** a weekend and a news lock say **different things** about why a bar is not tradeable, and one message for two causes is the hg-v940 defect.
- **What TAURIC deliberately does NOT get, and why:** the news mark is on its **card**, not in its forward record. A new ledger field needs the three hg-v955 seams (`hgFwdNormalize`, `hgFwdRecordScan` and the desk's own `.map()`) **and a reader** — and a field nothing reads is ornamental, which is hg-v955's own finding. Half-wiring it here would reproduce exactly the defect that pack exists to record.
- **GOLD SCALP now refreshes every 3 minutes** (`HG_GOLDSCALP_AUTO_MS`), asked for directly. The global sweep is untouched. **What that actually buys, said rather than implied:** this desk mints on **15m bars**, so a 3-minute cycle brings a **new closed bar on roughly one scan in five**. What moves on the other four is the **live half** — the spread lock, the live mark, DEAD-ON-ARRIVAL, the conviction evaluation and the hg-v930 one-at-a-time hold — the half that decides whether a card already on screen is still tradeable. Claiming five times the setups would be false; **five times the re-checks** is what this is. The bars are genuinely re-fetched: `hgCandleCacheTtl` returns **60s** on this foreground path, well inside 3 minutes.
- **It needs no lock of its own:** `goldscalpRefresh()` self-guards on `__scan.busy` and returns `'busy'`, so a tick landing on the global sweep or a manual RUN cannot double-scan, and `'busy'` is a normal outcome rather than a failure. **One page, one clock** (hg-v958): a second init installs no second timer. Fails open at every seam — no window, no `setInterval`, the module absent, or a refresh that rejects — and in none of them does the timer die, because a desk that stops refreshing silently is the defect hg-v958 exists to remove.
- **A stale claim in this file, found while adding that clock:** the hg-v441 entry said `HG_AUTO_REFRESH_HARDCODED_MS = HG_GLOBAL_SCAN_MS`, that the header showed **AUTO 10m**, and that OFF / 2m / 3m / 5m / 15m clicks were **ignored**. **hg-v656 set that constant to `0`** — unlocking the selector — and never corrected the entry, so for eight packs this file documented a lock the source had already removed. Corrected above.
- **No gate is loosened, no threshold moves, and no setup leaves any board that was not already leaving it.**
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-news-raw-minters.mjs`.

### The news gate reaches the three gold desks that record a TICKET — hg-v964
- **hg-v963 gave the gold news gate a shared route, and its own reporter then named SEVEN desks with no route at all.** Three of them record a **conditionally true ticket** into the forward ledger — `newgold.js:1260` / `:1622`, `omnigold1.js:1599`, `goldpro.js:836` — so a plan composed inside a **CPI / NFP / FOMC / GDP** window entered that ledger as **tradeable** and would be judged as one. That is the contamination hg-v949 measured for the weekend, arriving through the other calendar.
- **NEW GOLD and OMNIGOLD 1 reach formation through `hgGoldFormation`, so the lock goes THERE** — one rule, one home (hg-v949) — beside the weekend check and on the **same `opts.atMs`**, so the two calendars can never judge different moments. That drift is exactly what produced the hg-v952 wall-clock defect and its hg-v963 repeat, and it is now impossible **by construction** rather than by a caller remembering. **GOLD PRO** decides `ticket` at its own forward-record site, so it reads the gate at the **same bar** as its own weekend verdict, from **one** bar read serving both.
- **What is withheld and what is not:** only `tradable`, and on GOLD PRO only the **ticket flag**. The card keeps its confluence, its venue read and its evidence, and the forward row is **still recorded** — labelled `GP-NEWS-LOCKED` — so the population can be separated later (hg-v955). That is the hg-v552/v572 rule: a hard drop empties a board, a demote does not.
- **Why this withholds where the weekend only marks on GOLD PRO:** hg-v950 chose to mark there because withholding would have moved the board on evidence that pack had not gathered. A tier-1 news lock is a **different claim** — not that the edge is worse, but that new minting is barred — and the repo already treats it as a **hard block** on GOLD SCALP, GOLD SWING and OMNIGOLD, where `hgGoldInstFilter` rejects the candidate outright. Same policy, not a new one.
- **Measured on the real formation** with the repo's own fixture: a clear weekday instant **FORMS** and is tradable; **−30 min, −10 min, the release itself and +10 min** all stand aside with the reason **named**; **+3h** forms again — a gate that never opens is not a gate. **Fails open** at every seam (no instant, unreadable, epoch zero, no snapshot, `goldind.js` absent), each half of the pairing independently.
- **The reporter moves them:** seven uncovered → **four**, and the four still without a route are **named** rather than quietly dropped.
- **One survivor is an EQUIVALENT MUTANT and is reported as one:** removing the explicit `state` / `formed` / `tradable` assignment inside the lock leaves the initialiser values already in place and the branch returns immediately — proved identical on **ten** inputs, the same shape as the weekend block above it.
- **Two real gaps in this pack's own guard, caught by its own mutation pass:** testing the exported verdict proves the **rule**, not that the **desk** asks for it — so deleting the two GOLD PRO lines that read the gate and stash it on the plan left every assertion passing, and so did handing it a different series. The call site is now **lifted and executed** and the argument observed. **And one stale list in hg-v963's own guard:** it named four uncovered tabs by hand and this pack wired one of them, turning it red with nothing wrong. It now asserts the **property** — every uncovered desk genuinely has no route, every routed desk is not in that bucket — so covering a desk never again requires editing the guard.
- **No gate is loosened and no threshold moves.**
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-news-formation.mjs` (72 assertions; **13/14** behavioural mutations caught across `gold-formation.js` and `goldpro.js`, the fourteenth proved equivalent, baseline verified green before every pass).

### The gold news gate was DEFEATED by a literal, not missing — hg-v963
- **`hgGoldNewsGate` has locked new gold minting 30 min before / 15 min after CPI · NFP · FOMC · GDP since hg-v554, it is reached inside `hgGoldInstFilter`, and it WORKS** — driven on a real snapshot it locks at −10 min and +10 min around a CPI instant and releases by +3h.
- **GOLD ULTRA (2 call sites) and GOLD DIRECTION (3) called that gated mint and handed it a literal `news: null`.** On null the gate returns `{lock:false, unchecked:true}` — **fail-open by construction, every scan, forever** — and the snapshot they needed is a global (`window.hgNewsState`) their sibling **SUPER GOLD already reads**. Five literals disabled the one gate on this desk list that answers to the events that actually move gold.
- **The instant was wrong too:** both passed the **wall clock**, so a scan re-run after a release dated the lock wrong in both directions — the hg-v952 SUPER GOLD defect in a third place. Each lane now reads **its own signal bar** (15m scalp, 4h swing), because one shared instant would mislabel one of them (hg-v950), and the guard proves the two lanes get different instants from their own series.
- **The rule lives once**, in `gold-formation.js` beside the weekend calendar, and **delegates** to `hgGoldNewsGate` rather than re-deciding what a tier-1 event is — with `goldind.js` absent there is **no verdict** rather than a second news calendar (hg-v949). It fails **open** at every seam: no instant, an unreadable one, a negative one, **epoch zero** (hg-v953), no snapshot, or a snapshot the gate itself reads as **unchecked**. That last case is the one `news: null` actually produced, and it is asserted separately because a mutation treating it as not-locked survived the first pass.
- **A coverage reporter, DERIVED not typed:** the desk list comes from `HG_GOLD_WEEKEND_MINTERS`, the repo's one census of minting gold desks, because hg-v954 established that a hand-kept second copy inside a reporter is what goes stale — it had gone stale twice by then. The partition is checked against the **census count the reporter itself saw**, after the first cut of the guard hardcoded `15` against a census of `14`. And it **VERIFIES behaviourally**: the probe hands the shared verdict an instant inside a tier-1 window and one outside and requires the two to differ, so a route that **never** locks and one that **always** locks both read **BROKEN**.
- **Seven desks report UNCOVERED and the reporter names them rather than anyone's memory:** NEW GOLD, OMNIGOLD 1, OPTI GOLD, GOLD PRO, 80PERCENT, GOLD (inline), TAURIC — **more than the four** predicted from hg-v950's raw-bar minters. They are **reported and deliberately not wired**: withholding on a desk that never had the read changes what leaves the board, and this pack fixes what is broken before widening.
- **Five grep-satisfiable assertions in this pack's own guard, all caught by its own mutation pass:** counting the name `hgGoldNewsSnapshot` is satisfied by the `gfn()` lookup line alone, so deleting the assignment below it survived — as did reverting the instant to the wall clock and dropping the signal-bar read (three on GOLD ULTRA, two on GOLD DIRECTION). Both blocks are now **lifted out of the file and executed** under stubs (the hg-v951 technique) and the values read back.
- **Nothing is re-baked here** (403 CONNECT on every data route). **No gate is loosened, no threshold moves, and no setup leaves any board that was not already leaving it** — what changes is that two desks now **ask** the gate instead of answering for it.
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-news-gate-wire.mjs` (77 assertions, **19/19** behavioural mutations caught across `gold-formation.js`, `goldultra.js` and `golddirection.js`, baseline verified green before every pass).

### The GOLD SWING live blocks reproduce their own headline — hg-v961 measured the wrong thing (hg-v962)
- **This corrects my own previous pack.** hg-v916 gave every SCALP row a `live` block because the scalp headline is the whole 2,193-trade replay and the desk forms 1,205 of it — a real subset, and the gap is the point. hg-v961 gave the SWING rows a `live` block and described it the same way. **It is not the same thing.** On **all fourteen** swing rows the live block reproduces the headline to the headline's own precision, because the swing headline was **already** derived from `backtest-goldswing-results.json` before hg-v961 touched it — proved by booting the pre-hg-v961 tree, **14 of 14 identical**. So what hg-v961 actually delivered was **regeneration and provenance**, not a population correction, and its three "removals asserted at zero" were the **SCALP** desk's gates (cost bar, 1.5×ATR stop floor, suppress), none of which is this desk's.
