- **What the swing desk does that the walk does not is in the artifact's OWN metadata**, quoted rather than asserted: `hgFilterGoldPostGate`, the weekend demotes, and the best-levels / formation ticket batch — and **`hgApplyGoldBestLevels` REWRITES entry, stop and T1** on every live swing ticket. Those three numbers decide whether a trade fills, where it stops and what it wins. So every swing edge verdict is measured on a **pre-formation book**, and the population the desk forms is **unmeasured**.
- **The card stops claiming a filter that never ran.** The note read *"on the N trades the desk **still forms**"* over a population nothing was removed from, with a parenthetical about rows *"gated out before a card is minted"*. It now says there is **no separate live population**, that what the desk forms is **unmeasured**, and **names the best-levels pass** as the reason — the difference between an honest gap and a shrug. The SCALP note is untouched where the subset is real.
- **The duplication is now reportable rather than assumed:** **`hgGoldEdgeLiveIsHeadline`** and **`hgGoldEdgeLiveScope`** partition each desk — swing **14 duplicate, ZERO independent**; scalp **22 independent, 3 duplicate, 2 suppressed-none** — and the buckets partition the table in both directions. An unreadable table reports **null**, never a clean partition (hg-v954).
- **The obvious fix is MEASURED and REFUSED.** Scoping the walk to the tab's own R:R floor flips two rows' net sign — **`ob`** (n=4→3) and **`p8range`** (n=40→34, which would turn a neutral row that *can lead* into a demote) — and it is refused because the walk computes `rr` on the **inline engine's** levels while the floor is applied by the pass that **replaces** them. Filtering a pre-snap ratio by a post-snap floor is a **second wrong population**, not a correction. **No verdict moves.** `HG_GOLD_SWING_SCOPE` is generated by `scripts/swing-live-scope.mjs` (in the `gold:rebake` chain; `npm run gold:swing-scope` is the read-only drift check), and `hgGoldSwingScopeHtml` **branches on the verdict** so a future bake that *can* replay the formation pass announces itself rather than hiding behind today's refusal (hg-v935, hg-v944); it renders **nothing** without the literal.
- **A third finding — a documented gate that does not exist:** this file said swing formation enforced **2.0R** via `HG_GOLD_SWING_MIN_RR` (hg-v962 corrected it). The file says **1.5**, and `git log -S` finds no commit where it was ever 2. That false number sent the first cut of this pack measuring against 2.0R and reporting **72.8%** where the honest figure is **10.2%**. The generator **reads** the floor from `gold-best-levels.js` and is fatal without it, the guard drives it against a tree declaring a *different* floor so a retyped copy cannot follow, and it now holds this file's claim to the source.
- **Four defects in this pack's own work, all caught by its own mutation pass:** the identity predicate first **re-rounded** the live figure, which sends 0.2415 to 0.242 and calls a row that is the same number independent — it is a **tolerance at the headline's precision**, and the boundary case proving it had to be **0.2155**, because 0.2154 and 0.2145 both re-round back onto 0.215 and cannot tell the two rules apart; the flip rule reads **net**, and on this walk gross flips the same two rows so that mutation was **equivalent here** and is now proved on a synthetic artifact where gross stays positive while net crosses; the main-module check was a filename **suffix** test and this guard's own name ends with the same suffix, so importing the module ran the whole CLI report into the test output (fixed by comparing resolved paths, with the probe **named** to reproduce it); and the desk-wiring assertion was **grep-satisfiable**, passing while the call was deleted from the banner because the name survives on the lookup line above — it is now bounded to the banner assignment.
- **Nothing is re-baked here** (403 CONNECT on every data route) and the committed tree round-trips to **zero drift**. **No gate, threshold or verdict moves on any desk, and no setup leaves any board.**
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-swing-live-scope.mjs` (91 assertions, **22/22** behavioural mutations caught across `goldind.js`, `scripts/swing-live-scope.mjs` and `goldswing.js`, baseline verified green before every pass).

### GOLD SWING judged its edge on a headline n of 1, with its own 244-trade walk unread — hg-v961
- **hg-v916 gave every SCALP row a `live` block measured on the population that desk forms today, and hg-v960 made the verdict rule computable on it. The SWING half of the same table got neither.** Its fourteen rows carried the bridge replay headline `n` — **as low as 1** — and `scripts/backtest-goldswing-results.json`, the desk's own walk since hg-v574, was **read by nothing**. Two of those rows are **`prefer`**, which grants a +2 rank boost on GOLD SWING.
- **Every swing row now carries a live block**, derived by **`liveSwingPopulation`** from that walk: **244 settled trades**, largest row **n=44**, so the verdicts rest on populations of 1–44 rather than on a headline.
- **It is NOT the scalp derivation, and that distinction is the pack.** `goldswing` calls the edge gate with **`demoteOnly: true`**, so a suppressed kind still **forms** there and must not be removed from the population; the hg-v912 cost reject is not this desk's gate either. All three removals are **modelled and asserted at zero** — 0 under the cost bar, 0 under the 1.5×ATR stop floor, 0 for a suppress — and the bake prints **how many it checked**, because a removal silently skipped and a removal measured at zero look identical from outside (`assertSwingRemovalsModelled` returns the `checked` list the bake prints and the guard asserts, after a call-site bypass survived mutation invisibly).
- **Two of the four tiers are UNREACHABLE on this desk by arithmetic:** the scalp bars demand **n≥50** for both `prefer` and `suppress`, and the largest swing population is **44**. That is hg-v920's *tier the arithmetic cannot produce*, inside this table.
- **What is not recoverable is left `null` and NAMED.** The swing prefer floor is bounded by the table's own behaviour to **(23, 32]** — `ribbon` at n=23 is positive on both legs and is **not** preferred, `weekly` at n=32 is — and no value in that interval is written anywhere in the repo. Picking one would be fitting a bar to reproduce its own answers, which hg-v922 and hg-v935 refuse. So the rule returns **null** there, a fourth bucket **UNRECOVERABLE** is reported beside follows-the-rule / declared / undeclared, and **eight rows sit in it** rather than being quietly ruled on. `hgGoldEdgeTierInUse` keeps the two ways a bar can be missing apart: a tier rows **do** carry whose bar is unrecorded (swing `prefer`) is uncheckable; a tier **no** row carries (swing `suppress`) simply falls through.
- **A correction to my own first cut, found before shipping:** it gave swing its own demote floor of **n≥12**, taken from prose inside one row's `why` string. **Twelve is a bar FITTED to reproduce the rows it judges** — the table bounds the swing demote bar to **(2, 14]**, and 12 is a value picked inside it, which is exactly what the same pack refuses one field along for `prefer`. A bar searched to agree with the table it then validates is the same error with the search hidden. The demote bar is now **shared with scalp and has no sample floor at all**, and the single row where rule and table disagree — **`p6zfade` at n=2** — is **declared** on its own row in the same form as the hg-v928 refusals at n=6 and n=1. The guard proves **the interval a fitted bar would have hidden in** and that the shipped bar is outside it.
- **A real gap the hg-v959 provenance guard caught, not me:** the literal writer began reading the swing walk and **`npm run gold:rebake` never ran it** — a fresh orphan **one pack after** that fix. The chain now walks GOLD SWING, the walk resolves its output from the one shared constant in `lib/gold-artifacts.mjs` and answers `--print-out`, and **the `produced` set inside that guard is no longer hand-typed**: it is derived by **asking each `node` step of the chain what it writes**, because the hand-kept list inside the guard built to stop stale lists was itself the thing that went stale (hg-v952, one layer in).
- **Nothing is re-baked here** (403 CONNECT on every data route) and the committed tree still round-trips to **zero drift**. **No gate, threshold or verdict moves on any desk, and no setup leaves any board.**
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-swing-edge-rule.mjs` (136 assertions) and `tests/test-gold-artifact-provenance.mjs` (55 assertions), **30/30** behavioural mutations caught across `goldind.js`, `scripts/edge-live-population.mjs`, `scripts/rebake-gold-literals.mjs`, `scripts/backtest-goldswing.mjs` and `package.json`, baseline verified green before every pass.

### Gold edge verdicts: hand-typed on generated evidence — hg-v960
- **`HG_GOLD_SETUP_EDGE.scalp` decides suppress / demote / prefer for GOLD SCALP, GOLD SWING, GOLD ULTRA, GOLD DIRECTION and MILLI GOLD.** Every row carries a `live` block the generator writes and an `action` **a human typed**, and nothing kept the two in step — so a re-bake moved the evidence and left the decision frozen. That is hg-v946's hand-typed-prefer-book failure, inside the table hg-v928 retuned.
- **The rule was not merely unstated — it was UNCOMPUTABLE.** The bars are `suppress: live n≥50 ∧ live gross≤0 ∧ live net≤−0.20`, `prefer: live n≥50 ∧ live gross>0 ∧ live net≥+0.10`, `demote: live net<0`. Both the suppress and the prefer bar need a **gross on the live population**, and the live block emitted only `net`. **Four** rows carried a `liveGross` recorded *by hand* inside their hg-v928 `retune` blocks; for the other **twenty-one the number existed nowhere in the repo** — so every suppress and prefer verdict in force was unverifiable against the evidence shipped beside it.
- **The gross is computed now, and validated rather than assumed:** on the live population it reproduces all four hand-recorded values **exactly to 4dp** — `nyexh −0.1235`, `liqsweep 0.0313`, `bosalign 0.0654`, `ribbon 0.1093` — and in `goldind.js` the generated `live.gross` now sits one line above the `retune.liveGross` it matches. Rounded to 4dp rather than `net`'s 3dp, because that is the precision those four were recorded at.
- **The rule lives once, in code:** **`hgGoldEdgeVerdictFromLive`**. **No verdict moves** — applied to the shipped table it reproduces **23 of 25** in-force verdicts, and the two it does not are the two hg-v928 deliberately **refused**, on **n=6** and **n=1**, because the demote bar has no sample floor and tightening on the thinnest rows in the table is not an improvement. Both refusals are now **declared on their own rows** as `actionWhy` — data, not prose.
- **An UNDECLARED departure is fatal to the bake**, never a warning. **`hgGoldEdgeVerdictDepartures`** partitions every row into follows-the-rule / declared / undeclared, and `rebake-gold-literals.mjs` refuses to bake while the last list is non-empty, naming the row, the verdict in force and what the rule says. That is the hg-v921 rule — a generated thing the generator cannot account for stops the bake — applied to a **verdict** instead of a block.
- **Two mutations survived the first pass, both the same shape:** the two fatal branches **cannot be reached on a clean tree**, so a bucket nothing can land in is a rubber stamp. The enforcement is now an **exported function driven on injected inputs**, and both refusals are proved to fire and to name the offending row (hg-v937 hit this exact shape).
- **A third defect found by the suite, not by me:** the hg-v921 round-trip fixture corrupted a live block by matching a **fixed field list**, so adding `gross` silently stopped it matching and it corrupted nothing — caught only because that test asserts the fixture really changed something *first*. It matches by sample to the closing brace now. And its corrupted values read `prefer` against an in-force `demote`, which the new enforcement correctly refused; the fixture now changes the numbers **without contradicting the verdict**, because whether a literal round-trips and whether a verdict follows the rule are two different questions.
- **One survivor is an EQUIVALENT MUTANT and is reported as one:** removing the null-row guard leaves the function throwing into its own `catch` and returning `null` anyway — proved identical on nine inputs.
- **Nothing is re-baked here** (403 CONNECT on every data route) and the committed tree still round-trips to **zero drift**. **No gate, threshold or verdict moves on any desk, and no setup leaves any board.**
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-edge-verdict-rule.mjs` (57 assertions, **19/20** behavioural mutations caught across `goldind.js`, `scripts/rebake-gold-literals.mjs` and `scripts/edge-live-population.mjs`, the twentieth proved equivalent, baseline verified green before every pass).

### Gold re-bake wrote a file no literal reads — hg-v959
- **`npm run gold:rebake` could not refresh the evidence the gold desks form from, and reported success.** `scripts/backtest-goldscalp.mjs` wrote `backtest-goldscalp-results.json`; `scripts/rebake-gold-literals.mjs` derived **every** gold literal from `backtest-goldscalp-results-**floor**.json`; **nothing in the repo wrote that file**. Two halves of one pipeline each hardcoded a filename, and the filenames disagreed.
- **Proved both ways, not read off the imports:** deleting 80% of the trades from the file the walk writes left the drift check reporting *"every baked literal already equals its artifact"* — **zero drift** — while the same edit to the floor file moved **22 scalp rows and the walk span**.
- **What it cost beyond staleness, and this is the half that reaches setup formation:** the two artifacts are different **pipelines**, by their own metadata. The literals' source records *"goldRankSetups ctx carries **no** candle rows"* — its confluence scorer could not score. The file the walk writes records *"carries candle rows since hg-v700 (tab parity)"*. The live desk feeds rows at **`goldscalp.js:2328`**. So the suppress / demote / prefer table behind **GOLD SCALP, GOLD SWING, GOLD ULTRA, GOLD DIRECTION and MILLI GOLD** was measured on a ranking context that is not the desk's.
- **Measured with the repo's own population code, on both walks:** live book **n=1205 net −0.020** (literals' source) vs **n=1213 net −0.033** (tab-accurate); `ribbon` **+0.027 → +0.003** — the thinnest of the four verdicts hg-v928 flipped — and `hvn` −0.049 → −0.083. The live rows carry `n / net / oosHeld / oosBroke` and **no gross**, so only the `net<0` demote bar is evaluable, and on that bar **no verdict crosses**. The four rows that change (`vwap`, `nyexh`, `liqsweep`, `sweep`) lose a population they only had because that older walk suppressed them *after the fact*.
- **Nothing is re-baked here and no literal moves:** every data route is 403 CONNECT, so the walk cannot fetch bars. What ships is the **wiring**, so the next re-bake on a machine that can fetch refreshes what the desks actually read.
- **The fix is ONE HOME, not a checker.** **`lib/gold-artifacts.mjs`** holds the three gold artifact paths and the walk, the literal writer and `edge-live-population.mjs` all import them — the class of defect is now structural rather than detectable, the same reason hg-v949 keeps the gold calendar in one place and calls a second copy a second calendar.
- **The first cut was a checker, and it is deleted.** It walked the chain reporting artifacts read but never written; it found the defect, and then — wired into the head of the chain it inspects — **enumerated and spawned itself**, taking this machine to a load average over **1,000** with ~9,900 processes. A checker that validates a pipeline by *running* it also needs the live feed, so it is least usable on the machine that most needs it.
- **A third copy found while wiring it:** the filename is also the provenance **label** baked into `HG_GOLD_EDGE_WALK`, so it is derived from the same constant now and proved byte-identical by zero drift.
- **The walk can name its output:** `--out=` exists so the chain can be pointed, and **refuses** both ways it could recreate the overwrite incident the file's own comment records — a smoke run aimed at a real artifact, and a path that escapes `scripts/`. `--print-out` lets the guard **ask** each walk what it writes instead of parsing source for a string that `join()` and the `--symbol` branch defeat.
- **The name is historical, and is said to be:** `-floor` denotes no config — `meta.rules` is byte-identical between the two artifacts.
- **A weakness in this pack's own guard, caught by mutation:** comparing `REPLAY` to the constant passes whether the path is **imported or retyped to the same value**, so a mutation restoring the literal survived. The fix is hg-v956 discipline — a deliberately **textual** assertion that says so — with comments stripped first **in both directions**, because the honest files legitimately name the artifact in their headers and the surviving mutation wrote it as `'scripts/' + the name`.
- **No gate, threshold or verdict moves on any desk, and no setup leaves any board.**
- Tests: `tests/test-gold-artifact-provenance.mjs` (38 assertions, **16/16** behavioural mutations caught across `lib/gold-artifacts.mjs`, `lib/fs-trace.cjs`, `scripts/backtest-goldscalp.mjs` and `scripts/rebake-gold-literals.mjs`, baseline verified green before every pass).

### Build freshness poll — hg-v958
- **There are TWO update paths, and hg-v957 fixed one of them.** Reported from the desk a second time: *v951 STILL ON THIS*.
- **`build-stamp.js` already carried a complete staleness detector** — `hgBuildFreshness()` fetches its own source cache-busted, parses the version the server is serving, compares, paints **STALE** and reloads. Its reload guard is keyed **per live version**, so it never had the hg-v957 lockout bug at all. It should have recovered a pinned tab on its own, every time.
- **It ran once.** `hgBuildInit` asked the server at `DOMContentLoaded` — the one moment a tab is current by definition — and after that only on `visibilitychange`. This desk is built to sit open all day as the **foreground** tab (`HG_GLOBAL_SCAN_MS` re-scans it every ten minutes without ever hiding it), so `visibilitychange` **never fires**. **Measured by driving the real file before the fix: zero timers installed, one fetch at boot, none in the eight hours after**, and a second only after a tab switch. That is the hg-v957 defect in the other path.
- **`HG_BUILD_POLL_MS` = 5 min**, deliberately the fastest of the three clocks (service-worker update 15 min, desk re-scan 10 min): it is one small GET of one file, and it is the path that can recover a tab whose service worker is wedged.
- **Second defect, and the reason a clock could not simply be added:** the probe is cache-busted with `Date.now()`, and a cache entry is keyed by the **full URL** — so **every probe wrote its own permanent entry** under a key nothing can ever read back (**measured: three probes, three distinct entries**). `activate()` deletes only caches under a *different* `HG_CACHE` name, so on a tab pinned to one build the version never bumps and they are never cleared — **the leak is worst exactly when the desk is stuck**. Polling 288 times a day without fixing it would be a storage leak, not a fix. `hgNeverCache` now refuses to **write** any request the caller marked `cache: 'no-store'`, which is what no-store means.
- **What that rule costs, named and proved:** the seven `./data/*.json` files are fetched `no-store` too, so they stop being backfilled at runtime. All seven are in `HG_SHELL`, and the guard drives an **offline** `no-store` read and requires the precached copy to still come back. An offline **probe** must *not* be answered from cache — a stale version read as the live one would say *fresh* and pin the tab — and that is asserted too.
- **New risk this pack carries:** polling makes the automatic reload ~100× more frequent, so it must not land on someone typing (OMNIGOLD 1 takes a hand-pasted DATA BLOCK). Focus in an `input` / `textarea` / `select` / contenteditable **defers** the reload — and defers rather than loses it: the per-version key is written **after** that check, so the next tick reloads the moment focus leaves the field. **Fails open** — no document, no `activeElement`, or a document that throws, and the reload proceeds, because refusing an update on an unreadable guard is a new lockout of exactly the kind hg-v957 removed.
- **One page, one clock:** a second `hgBuildInit()` installs no second timer and no second listener.
- **A vacuous assertion of my own, caught by mutation:** `hgBuildEditingNow(null)` falls back to the global `document`, so asserting it inside a harness that *has* one never reaches the no-document branch and a fail-**closed** mutation survived. It is now driven in a context with no document at all.
- **A seventh grep-satisfiable guard this session:** `tests/test-build-stamp.mjs` proves `build-stamp.js` is precached with `sw.indexOf("'./build-stamp.js'")`, which is satisfied by the entry appearing inside a **comment** — commenting the shell line out survives it, exactly like the hg-v957 localhost check that matched the word in a comment. `install()` is now driven and the cache asked what it actually holds.
- **Not verified here:** this environment gets HTTP 403 on the production origin, so `npm run check:prod` cannot read what Render serves and **no claim is made about whether the deploy landed**. What is fixed is the browser half — both paths of it.
- Tests: `tests/test-build-freshness-poll.mjs` (65 assertions, **21/21** behavioural mutations caught across `build-stamp.js` and `sw.js`, baseline verified green first).

### Service worker update reload — hg-v957
- **A tab got ONE automatic reload, ever, and then stayed pinned to the build it booted with.** Reported from the desk: still on **hg-v951** with hg-v956 deployed.
- **The service worker was never the problem.** `sw.js` calls `skipWaiting()` on install and `clients.claim()` on activate, and every version bumps `HG_CACHE`, so the new worker installs, activates and claims the page. What did not happen is the **reload** — and without it the page keeps executing the JavaScript it parsed at first load, so the version badge keeps reading the old `build-stamp.js`.
- **The guard was a LOCKOUT, not a loop guard:** it wrote `sessionStorage['hg_sw_reload'] = '1'` the first time a tab took a new worker and returned early on every later `controllerchange`. `sessionStorage` lives for the life of the **tab**, across reloads — and this desk auto-refreshes every 10 min and is built to sit open all day, so the key was never cleared.
- **A loop is a question about elapsed time.** The key now holds the **timestamp** of the last automatic reload; a claim is honoured when it is old enough (**60 s** window — 1 s and 59 s later do not reload, 61 s does). A legacy `'1'` reads as 1970 and is therefore old, so **an already-pinned tab recovers on its next update** rather than staying stuck.
- **Second defect:** `reg.update()` ran **once**, at registration — a tab open for a trading day never asked again, so no new worker was ever discovered to claim it. It now re-checks every **15 min**.
- **Fails open** at every seam: absent / empty / non-numeric / negative stored value, or `sessionStorage` throwing (private mode, blocked site data) → reload. A throwing `reg.update()` kills neither the listener, the reload, nor the timer.
- **The guard lifts the real block out of `index.html` and executes it** under a fake `navigator` / `sessionStorage` / `location` (the hg-v951 technique). The block had **no test of any kind** before this.
- **Not verified here:** this environment gets HTTP 403 on the production origin, so `npm run check:prod` cannot read what Render serves and **no claim is made about whether the deploy landed**. What is fixed is the browser half, which pins a tab to an old build even after a correct deploy.
- Tests: `tests/test-sw-update-reload.mjs` (26 assertions, **9/9** behavioural mutations caught in `index.html`, baseline verified green first).
- **A fifth fixed-width source slice, caught by this pack's own suite run:** `tests/test-tabs.mjs` read a fixed **1,500-character** window before `serviceWorker.register('sw.js')` to check the guards, so the comment added above them pushed `typeof navigator` / `location.protocol` out of range and turned it red **with the guards untouched and correct**. It now bounds the read to the **inline block** that holds the registration. And the localhost check was satisfied by the word appearing in the **comment** above it — deleting the real `location.hostname === 'localhost'` test survived mutation — so it now matches the comparison rather than the word (3/3 on genuine removals).


- **What the gold weekend actually costs the edge table, and the gate that was already paying for it — with two corrections to my own hg-v953 (hg-v956):** **(a) The wrong file.** `scripts/` holds **three** goldscalp artifacts and they are not interchangeable: the table's `live` column is computed by `edge-live-population.mjs`, which reads **`backtest-goldscalp-results-floor.json`** (2,605 trades), and AGENTS.md's own `HG_GOLD_EDGE_WALK` names that span. hg-v953 quoted the 2,575-trade neighbour and called it *"what `HG_GOLD_SETUP_EDGE` is measured on"*. On the right file the share is **27.2%**, and the **set of verdicts that would move is different** (`asian` and `ribbon` in, `hvn` and `p8range` out) — so the file mattered, and the correction is an import rather than a sentence. **(b) The wrong basis.** The headline `n`/`gross`/`net` on every row are whole-book and contaminated; the **`live`** figures hg-v916 added and hg-v928 acted on are not — **7 weekend rows in 1,205, 0.6%**, nothing to scope. **The mechanism, measured rather than assumed, is the finding:** a weekend PAXG bar is a thin crypto proxy with almost no range, so ATR collapses, so the stop is narrow — and the **hg-v912 cost reject removes narrow stops**. Median stop **0.038%** weekend against **0.266%** open (a factor of seven) against a **0.16%** bar; **98.4%** of weekend rows fail that gate against **6.4%** of open rows. **That gate has been paying for the calendar since it shipped and nobody had established it in either direction.** **Nothing moves, for two independent reasons:** every one of the seven moving rows is **thin** under the desk's own bar (four disjoint windows at both fill bounds on win AND gross AND net, imported from `factor-separation` / `demote-separation` rather than rebuilt), the pooled read is thin too — precisely because the cost gate already removed the cohort — and the live basis has nothing left to correct. hg-v920 refused three loosenings on this evidence, hg-v944 a fourth, hg-v945 reported five leans without acting on one. **A contaminated measurement is not a measurement of the opposite; it is a measurement nobody has made.** **And the fourth seam:** hg-v955 found `goldShut` dropped by `hgFwdNormalize`, `hgFwdRecordScan` and the desks' own `.map()`; **the replay row builders dropped it too** — the place hg-v953 actually named as its reason for marking — so seven scripts read these artifacts and exactly one carried a calendar of its own. Both walks now carry the mark from the candidate onto the trade at **every** build site (live book **and** EDGE-suppressed shadow book) and emit it from `settleRecord` on hg-v955's three-state rule; **a single-site check passed while the other site silently lost the mark**, and mutation caught exactly that. **Honest limit, stated in the guard:** the committed artifacts carry no marks yet because a re-bake needs bars this environment cannot fetch, so every figure here is derived from `tISO` through the repo's own DST-aware calendar, which the artifact *does* carry; the script **calls** that calendar and the guard asserts it holds none of its own. One assertion is deliberately **textual** and says so — whether a number was imported or retyped is a property of the source, and no runtime comparison can tell them apart while the two agree, which is exactly when the drift starts. **No gate, threshold or verdict moves on any desk, and no setup leaves any board.** Re-derive: `node scripts/gold-edge-tradeable.mjs`. Tests: `tests/test-gold-edge-tradeable.mjs` (68 assertions, **17/17** behavioural mutations caught across `scripts/gold-edge-tradeable.mjs`, `scripts/backtest-goldscalp.mjs` and `scripts/backtest-goldswing.mjs`, baseline verified green before every pass).
- **The gold calendar mark was written by eight files and read by none — and the forward ledger discarded it at the door (hg-v955):** hg-v952 marked TAURIC's rows, hg-v953 the GOLD SCALP / GOLD SWING mints, hg-v954 GOLD DIRECTION's and GOLD ULTRA's ticket rows. Every one of those packs justified **marking rather than withholding** on the same grounds: the rows need the mark so the population can be **separated later**. **The separation was impossible.** `hgFwdNormalize` builds an explicit fifteen-key record and `goldShut` was not among them, so every mark was dropped on the way into storage; `hgFwdRecordScan` had its own explicit field list and dropped it a second time; and GOLD SCALP / GOLD SWING **rebuild** the row inside a `.map()`, dropping it a third time — so v953's mint mark never left the candidate either. That is hg-v932's finding (*"the work was done and dropped on the way out"*), this time in my own packs, and the precedent sits **three fields above the hole**: `mark` was accepted-but-never-forwarded for exactly this reason, with the comment saying so still in place. **The field now survives all three seams, and a reader exists**, because a mark nothing reads is ornamental: **`hgFwdGoldCalendarSplit`** reports the live split and **`hgFwdGoldCalendarHtml`** renders one line through `hgGoldFwdNote`, the one seam every gold desk already paints. The aggregate folds a **tradeable** and a **weekend** bucket beside `gateClear` and `shown`, for the reason those fold — the live list is capped and pruned, so a split that lives only there is unanswerable past about four weeks. **Three states, not two, is the design point:** every record written before this carries no mark, and reading *not-shut* as *tradeable* would count the whole legacy ledger as gold-open — the same error one layer out, and exactly what made NEW GOLD's record read positive (hg-v949). `undefined` means **NOT RECORDED**, is counted and named as its own bucket, and never folds into either side; the panel renders **nothing** until a record carries the mark, because a line reading "0 weekend trades" on a ledger that never recorded the field is the false reassurance this pack exists to remove. **A hazard in my own rule, found by mutation:** the first cut wrote `rec.goldShut === true`, which turns a **truthy non-boolean into `false` — gold-OPEN** — so this field's own error would have arrived by coercion instead of by omission; the mirror (`!!`) reads the string `'no'` as shut. **Only the two booleans** are accepted now, proved on `1`, `0`, `'no'`, `''` and `{}`. **A second defect in this pack's own guard:** the fixture mutated the rows `hgFwdRecords` hands back, and that function re-parses storage on every call, so the settle never stuck and **two assertions passed on `null === null`**; the settled state is written back under the module's own key and the settled **count is asserted first**. **And a third:** the aggregate section **grepped** `hg-forward.js` for the two fold lines — the brittle shape corrected four times this session, and one that cannot see whether a branch is reachable; **`hgFwdFold` is exported and called**, with the three buckets required to **partition** the rows. **Nothing is gated on any of it:** a weekend-formed row is still recorded, still a ticket, still resolved, levels untouched. **No gate, threshold or verdict moves on any desk, and no setup leaves any board.** Tests: `tests/test-gold-forward-calendar.mjs` (68 assertions, **19/19** behavioural mutations caught across `hg-forward.js`, `goldscalp.js`, `goldswing.js` and `gold-forward-read.js`, baseline verified green before every pass).
- **The coverage reporter's desk list was itself HAND-TYPED — the mechanism that produced its two previous blind spots (hg-v954):** hg-v951 added the inline GOLD tab to that list by noticing; hg-v952 added TAURIC by noticing. Each was written up as a blind spot fixed, and the thing that produced both — **a desk list kept by hand, inside the reporter built so gaps name themselves** — was never touched. The shell's GOLD nav group holds **seventeen** tabs; the reporter listed **twelve**. The five missing: MILLI GOLD, GOLD DIRECTION, GOLD ULTRA, GOLD SPOT, GOLD COINT. **Two of them write `ticket: true` XAUUSD rows into the forward ledger with no weekend reference of any kind** — stronger than the TAURIC case hg-v952 wired, whose rows record `ticket:false`, so there was no ticket to withhold. These record **tickets**, so a weekend-formed pick entered the ledger as tradeable and would be judged as one: hg-v949's contamination, in two more ledgers. **GOLD ULTRA's only two mentions of the word are prose in its walk metadata**, saying its universe carries *"24/7 weekend bars a broker never printed"* — the desk documented the contamination and did nothing about it. Both **mark and withhold nothing** (TAURIC precedent), keyed to their own signal bar (1h for GOLD DIRECTION, 15m for GOLD ULTRA) rather than the wall clock, proved by driving each under a wall clock on the *opposite* side of the calendar. **The other three are classified, not wired, and that distinction is the honest part:** MILLI GOLD **inherits OMNIGOLD** — since hg-v938 it renders the cards OMNIGOLD has already evaluated rather than scanning, so a second calendar here would be a second calendar; GOLD SPOT is a spot-perp basis monitor and GOLD COINT's own header says **CONTEXT only, no spread execution path**. That last claim is **asserted behaviourally**, not assumed: each is booted, refreshed, and required to write no forward record at all. **The census cannot go stale silently again:** every gold tab is classified as minter or non-minter, and the guard reads `HG_NAV_GROUPS` out of `index.html` by **executing** the declaration rather than grepping it (the hg-v951 lesson, after four brittle source-slice guards this session), requiring the lists to agree **in both directions** — nothing unclassified, nothing classified that the shell does not list, no tab in both buckets. **`hgGoldWeekendCensusGaps()`** reports it at runtime, and an **unreadable registry returns null**, never an empty gap list, because an unreadable census is not a clean one; the guard proves the reporter is not a rubber stamp by pushing a synthetic gold tab and requiring it to be named. **A defect this pack's own guard found in this pack's own wiring:** the coverage probe wants *truthy-when-shut / null-when-open*, so a route that cannot tell two known instants apart reads BROKEN — but the **mark** needs three states (shut, open, **unreadable**), and the probe shape makes the last two identical. Both desks were written with the probe shape, so **every Wednesday row silently carried no mark at all**. The full verdict is read for the mark and wrapped for the probe. **Four mutations survived the first GOLD ULTRA pass, all one gap:** the guard exercised its exported helpers and never its **real record path** — the hg-v952 TAURIC failure exactly. The shipped statement is now **lifted out of the file and executed** under stubs inside the module's own context, so deleting the mark outright is caught. **No gate, threshold or verdict moves on any desk, and no setup leaves any board.** Tests: `tests/test-gold-census.mjs` (77 assertions, **25/25** behavioural mutations caught across `gold-formation.js`, `golddirection.js` and `goldultra.js`, baseline verified green before every pass).

The swing walk's own artifact meta lists **"weekend demotes"** among the runScan stages it does **not** replay, and both walks run on PAXGUSDT, whose universe line in the same meta reads *"24/7 weekend bars a broker never printed"*. **Measured, and it is a quarter of the biggest gold evidence base in the repo:** **679 of GOLD SCALP's 2,575** committed trades (**26.4%**) formed inside the gold weekend — and that walk is what **`HG_GOLD_SETUP_EDGE`**, the table deciding suppress / demote / prefer on that desk, is measured on. The **rejected side-channel is marked too**, because that is the channel the replay settles *with* the plan. **A sixth instance of the `+null === 0` trap, in the shared calendar itself:** `''` and `0` both coerce to 0, which is **1970-01-01, a Thursday** — so an unreadable instant read as *gold was open* and marked a candidate accordingly, silently. - **The coverage reporter reported what its AUTHOR TYPED (hg-v952):** hg-v949 built it so gaps would name themselves and hg-v951 fixed its first blind spot — but **every `via` string in it was a claim nothing ever checked**, which is hg-v946's hand-typed-prefer-book failure living inside the reporter built to stop gaps hiding. - **The desk called plain GOLD is INLINE in the shell, and that is why nothing found it (hg-v951):** Every other gold desk is a module file, so every file-level survey could enumerate them — and so could hg-v949's coverage reporter, which finds a desk by probing a module global. **`runGold` is ~200 lines inside `index.html`**, registered as `id:'gold'` in `HG_NAV_GROUPS` beside the other sixteen. A reporter built so gaps name themselves had a blind spot, and the tab hiding in it was the one being asked about by name. **What it does:** reads `getXAUCandles` (chain falls back to Delta XAUTUSD and Binance PAXG, both 24/7), runs **two** gated lanes — swing on 4h, scalp on 15m — and **each calls `logSetup` and renders SEND TO TRADE PLAN and ADD TO BOOK**. So a setup formed on a Saturday bar was directly bookable from this tab. **Per lane**, because the lanes read different series: judging the 15m scalp by the 4h clock would mislabel it, and a mutation making the scalp lane read `h4` is caught. **Both handoffs go together, and nothing else goes at all:** hg-v930 is explicit that a demote alone does **not** remove those CTAs — hg-v611 needed a suppress verdict for exactly that, after a demote stamp left four stop-outs bookable — so when gold was shut the two CTAs are **replaced by the reason**, while the ledger, the evidence and the levels stay put. **Fails OPEN** at every seam. **The reporter can no longer be blind to an inline desk:** it is listed, probes the function the shell defines, the buckets still partition the list with it included, and with the shell absent it reports **NOT LOADED** rather than silently covered. **The guard EXECUTES the shipped code rather than grepping for it** — the first cut of three sections grepped `index.html` for the branch, the brittle shape corrected four times this session and one that cannot see whether a branch is reachable; each lane's real assignment statement is now **lifted out of the shell and run** under stubs, and the HTML it produces is read. Two fixture defects fell out of doing that honestly: the lift began *below* the verdict declaration the statement closes over, so it could not run; and an assertion searched the output for the words "SEND TO TRADE PLAN", **which the withholding notice itself contains** because it names what it removes — it failed against correct code until it asserted on the button markup instead. **No gate, threshold or verdict moves on any other desk, and no setup leaves any board.** Tests: `tests/test-gold-inline-weekend.mjs` (29 assertions, **8/8** behavioural mutations caught across `index.html` and `gold-formation.js`, baseline verified green first).
- **A session rule that admits the weekend, and the four desks hg-v949 could not reach (hg-v950):** v949 wired gold's trading calendar into the shared formation, and **its own coverage reporter named who was left**: OPTI GOLD, GOLD PINE, GOLD PRO and 80PERCENT mint from **raw bars** and never route through it. All four are covered now. **The headline is not a missing rule but a false premise.** 80PERCENT *has* a session rule — `hg80InSession` — and it reads the **UTC hour only, never the day**, so a Saturday 14:00 bar passes its 13:00–18:00 "NY session" test; measured against the repo's own calendar, **40 of the 672 hours** in a 28-day span pass that test **while gold is shut**. **That was a deliberate, documented choice, and half of it still stands:** the file said a weekday condition would be a silent deviation from the supplied spec — true, and **the firing rule is not touched**. It also said it "would change nothing on real data because gold prints no weekend bars" — and *that* is false for the feed this desk reads. Bars arrive through `hgOgFetchRows`, whose chain ends in `binanceKlines('PAXGUSDT')`, a **24/7** Binance pair, and that leg fires whenever XM is not configured (the default). NEW GOLD's committed walk proves it is not hypothetical: 9 of its 19 trades formed inside the gold weekend on PAXGUSDT (hg-v949). **So the spec decides what FIRES and the calendar decides what the desk PRESENTS AS TRADEABLE.** **Each desk is stamped at the instant its own shape makes correct, and the four differ** — one copied shape would have been wrong on three of them. **OPTI GOLD** walks 400–600 bars and emits breaks from across that window, so it stamps **per setup** from the setup's own break bar (the guard proves a single scan carries **both** shut and open setups, which a per-scan stamp could not do), and a shut-market break loses its **ACTIONABLE** claim while keeping ENTRY / STOP / T1. **80PERCENT** walks bars too and stamps **per signal**. **GOLD PINE**'s setups carry no instant at all, so it stamps **per mode** from each mode's own series (4h swing, 15m scalp) — one instant for both would mislabel one. **GOLD PRO** composes one plan from the last closed bar in hand, so a single instant is right there. **Everything fails OPEN:** no calendar, an unreadable instant or an empty series and the verdict is null and nothing is withheld — proved on all four. Nothing is deleted anywhere. **The coverage reporter now routes all four**, each with its route *named* rather than a bare flag; and since every desk on the list now has a route, the `uncovered` bucket would be unreachable — so the guard pushes a **synthetic routeless desk** and requires it to still report UNCOVERED, because a bucket nothing can land in is a rubber stamp. **One vacuity caught by mutation, again:** the first cut of the 80PERCENT section ran a random tape that fired **zero** signals and passed through its own `else` branch, and three mutations survived; the tape is now built to fire and the guard asserts the fire count is non-zero **first**. A second fixture bug surfaced the same way — sections 6 and 7 anchored the tape's *start* on a Saturday, putting the last bar thirteen days later on a weekday. **No gate, threshold or verdict moves on any other desk, and no setup leaves any board.** Tests: `tests/test-gold-weekend-minters.mjs` (39 assertions, **16/16** behavioural mutations caught across five files, baseline verified green first).
- **Gold is shut on the weekend, and the desks routing through the shared formation had never heard of it (hg-v949):** XAUUSD prints no bar from **Friday 22:00 UTC to Sunday 22:00 UTC** (DST-aware). The repo has encoded that since hg-v186 as `hgInGoldWeekend`, and OMNIGOLD has **vetoed** inside it since hg-v420 — **NEW GOLD and OMNIGOLD 1 minted straight through**. **It is live, not only historical:** `getXAUCandles` walks a feed chain ending in Delta XAUTUSD and Binance PAXG, both **24/7 crypto tokens**, so on a Saturday a desk on that chain is handed bars and mints an XAUUSD ticket at a price no broker quoted. **Measured, and it is half the record:** NEW GOLD's only settled walk runs on PAXGUSDT, and the artifact's own meta says what that means — *"24/7 weekend bars a broker never printed"*. Judged with the repo's own DST-aware rule rather than a re-implementation, **9 of its 19** trades formed inside the gold weekend, and the desk's entire positive reading is those nine (**+0.2403R** net at XM); the **tradeable** subset is **n=10, gross +0.0000R, net −0.0568R, win 40.0%** against exactly the **40.0%** its own 1.5R ladder demands. 15 of 33 fireLog fires are weekend fires. **No verdict is claimed from n=10 and none is offered** — the finding is that the quoted figure was not measured on a population the desk can trade, *not* that the desk loses; the gross reading exactly 0.0000R over ten trades is a small-sample coincidence and is said to be one. Re-derive: `node scripts/gold-weekend-population.mjs`, which **calls** the repo's calendar (and contains no calendar arithmetic of its own — the guard asserts that) and **quotes** the artifact's universe line rather than asserting the claim itself. **The rule lives once**, in `gold-formation.js`, and **delegates** to `hgInGoldWeekend`: a second copy of a calendar is a second calendar, and the guard proves that with `indicators2.js` absent the verdict is **null** rather than falling back to its own idea of a weekend. **The instant is the SIGNAL BAR, never `Date.now()`** — a scan re-run on Monday over Friday's bars must give Friday's answer, and the guard drives the same call under two wall clocks to prove it does not move; callers pass `atMs`, with deliberately no rows fallback because the desks here make an identical rows-less call by design. **It withholds `tradable` and nothing else** — confluence, venue and evidence reads all survive and the card stands aside rather than being deleted (hg-v552/v572: a hard drop empties a board, a demote does not). **Fails OPEN at every seam:** no `atMs`, an unreadable one, or the calendar absent, and the setup stands exactly as before — a calendar the desk cannot read is not a reason to withhold a setup. **A coverage reporter, because a list kept in prose goes stale silently (hg-v945):** `hgGoldWeekendCoverage()` probes the live globals and **partitions** the desk list into covered / uncovered / not-loaded, and a desk absent from the page reports **NOT LOADED**, never "covered". **OPTI GOLD, GOLD PINE, GOLD PRO and 80PERCENT report UNCOVERED** — they mint from raw bars and do not route through the shared formation, so they are named by the reporter rather than by anyone's memory. **One honest gap, recorded in the guard rather than in prose:** OMNIGOLD 1's call site is wired identically to NEW GOLD's, but reaching it needs a fixture clearing its 4-family spread rule, which this one does not — the first cut asserted over an **empty array** and passed vacuously, so reachability is asserted **first** and the rest is conditional on it, with the engine required to have RUN and declined for a NAMED reason. **A fifth `+null === 0` instance, this time in the guard itself:** a finiteness check let a lane passing `null` read as wired, and mutation caught it. **No gate, threshold or verdict moves on any other desk, and no setup leaves any board.** Tests: `tests/test-gold-weekend-formation.mjs` (46 assertions, **13/13** behavioural mutations caught across `gold-formation.js` and `newgold.js`, baseline verified green first).
- **OPTI GOLD scored the EXIT of every setup under an INFINITE horizon, on setups that all expire (hg-v948):** Working the gold desk list again, OPTI GOLD is the one minting gold desk with **no gold-native filter of any kind** (no news gate, no session rule, no inst filter, no cost gate, no measured-edge veto) and **no walk artifact** — every other minting gold desk has one. The defect is in the number it **ranks** by. `ogProb` scores two legs: the **fill** leg has always been distance- and horizon-aware (reflection principle on the bars remaining), while the **outcome** leg was the constant **1/(1+rr)** — gambler's ruin between two absorbing barriers, which is the answer *given that one of them is reached at all*. These setups die at `horizonBars` whether or not either was, so that constant is an **upper bound**, never the value — and it was also blind to **how far** the target is. Driven through the real function: a target **20×ATR away with 1 bar left** and one **0.5×ATR away with 40 bars** both scored `0.333`, and the desk rated the hopeless one **0.307** against 0.329. One estimator applied to one leg and omitted from the other. **The direction needs no measurement, only arithmetic:** the target sits rr R away and the stop 1R, so truncating at a finite horizon removes winners at least as readily as losers, and the figure can only be too high. **Quantified on the repo's only real gold book** (9,897 OMNIGOLD plans at the same 2R): at a **16-bar** horizon winners are censored more than losers in **4 of 4** disjoint windows (30.1/22.0 · 30.8/23.3 · 26.7/22.7 · 24.3/17.3), but at **32 and 48 bars it is 3 of 4 — not unanimous**. So the sign is arithmetic and the **magnitude carries no verdict**: no number from that walk is baked here or gated on, and that population is OMNIGOLD's gates, not this rule's. **The fix is the file's own estimator on both legs:** P(win|fill) becomes `min(1/(1+rr), P(travel to the target within the bars that remain))` — the same reflection form, stated once (`ogTravelP` / `ogCondBound`) instead of written twice. It is a **bound made of two bounds**, so it can only *lower* the number and no setup can be made to look better than this desk already claimed; across **360** distance × horizon × R:R combinations it never exceeds the old figure and never leaves [0,1]. Where the horizon is ample it returns **exactly** the old value and nothing moves. **What the change makes worse, said on the card:** the driftless assumption used to bite only the fill leg over short distances and now applies over long ones, where a driftless model is *least* like gold — so the **level** is a bound that may sit far below the truth; what survives is the **ordering**, which is all this number is used for, and the old figure stays on the row as `condInf` so the reader sees both and the gap. **Three instances of the same `+null === 0` trap, all found by this pack's own checks and the existing suite:** an absent `t1` read as a target **575×ATR** from a $2300 entry and produced a confident bound of **zero**; an absent `barsLeft` in `ogTravelP` read as *expired*; and the pre-existing resting guard did the same. **ABSENT is now distinct from EXPIRED everywhere** — unreadable yields no estimate, or falls back to the infinite-horizon figure, never a smaller number invented in its place. **Measured effect** on the real rule over 60 scans: the horizon binds on **75.0%** of resting estimates and changes nothing on the other **25.0%**; median p **0.061 → 0.046**; the lane leader changes on odds alone in **2 of 16** contests. **The tape is SYNTHETIC** — every data route here is 403 CONNECT — so that is weak evidence of *frequency* and is not offered as more; the behaviour is proved by driving the real `ogSignals` / `ogProb` / `ogTopPicks`. **Nothing leaves the board:** `ogProb` ranks and renders, it never mints, and no gate, threshold or verdict moves on any desk. Tests: `tests/test-optigold-horizon-bound.mjs` (34 assertions, **12/12** behavioural mutations caught, baseline verified green first).
- **GOLD DIRECTION crowns on a prefer row whose own twin says the opposite, and half its proof book is that row (hg-v947):** This tab crowns **only measured-proven** candidates, and hg-v702 built that proof at scan time from the live surfaces rather than a hardcoded copy. But branch (a) admitted **every** `HG_GOLD_SETUP_EDGE` prefer row without asking whether that row's exact OMNIGOLD twin is past the measured-failure bar — the disagreement hg-v943 acts on for GOLD SCALP / GOLD SWING (withholds the rank boost) and hg-v946 for GOLD ULTRA (withholds the crown). This desk went on crowning. **Measured, and it is half the book:** of the **four** prefer rows the live table carries, **two are `p9volbar`** — SCALP and SWING — whose exact twin **P9-VOLBAR** reads **−0.499R net at XM over 274 gate-clear trades, −2.97σ**, past the bar at which OMNIGOLD refuses to ticket a mechanic at all. The other two are `p6fail` (twin **not** past the bar, +0.73σ) and `weekly` (no twin). So the rule bites on exactly the rows it should and no others, and the guard **asserts that from the live table** rather than claiming it. **It withholds the CROWN and nothing else:** the candidate still forms, keeps its ENTRY / STOP / T1, keeps its prefer tier, still paints as a full card, and is neither demoted, suppressed, held nor rejected — proved by reading the snapshot and the rendered levels back. **Two seams, because one would have been a silent no-op:** `provenOf` short-circuits on the candidate's **own** prefer row and never consults the proven set, so fixing `buildProvenSet` alone would have changed nothing for most candidates — the hg-v945 trap exactly, and a mutation says so out loud. Both carry the rule and agree by construction (a withheld key is also absent from the set, so the list lookup finds nothing either). **The set is never allowed to shrink in silence (hg-v940):** the withheld rows are counted, named with **both** samples, printed on the stat line and published on the snapshot beside the set; and the snapshot `reason` plus the **NOT CROWNED** header now tell a candidate nothing has measured apart from one carrying a prefer row its own twin contradicts — saying "all silent on them" of the second is simply false, one message for two causes, the v940 failure in the header this time. **Scoped on purpose:** the `omnigold-prefer` branch **is** the OMNIGOLD record and has nothing to disagree with, and the `live-paid` branch is this tab's own forward ledger on its own gates and horizons — a third population, measured live, which a stale twin does not speak for. Both are proved untouched. **One lever, not a third switch:** `hgGoldSetTwinCheck(false)` reverses it here too, read through `hgGoldTwinCheckOn()`. **Fails OPEN at every seam** — OMNIGOLD absent, no verdict, or a throwing reader, and the row crowns as before. **The disclosure is goldind's ONE definition, not a second copy:** `hgGoldTwinConflictNote` now takes what the caller withholds, so this desk says **the CROWN** where the others say a rank boost, and the two existing callers pass two arguments and get byte-identical text. **No gate, threshold or verdict moves on any desk, and no setup leaves any board.** Tests: `tests/test-gold-direction-twin-crown.mjs` (51 assertions, **12/12** behavioural mutations caught across two files, baseline verified green first).
- **GOLD ULTRA crowned a mechanic the other gold desks refuse to promote, on the same evidence (hg-v946):** Working the gold desk list again, the hole this time is not a missing mechanic but a **disagreement**. hg-v943 measured that several mechanics on these tabs are **wired OMNIGOLD mechanics**, so each carries **two** records — and for **`p9volbar`** they disagree in sign with the tab holding the smaller sample: the tab row reads **+0.155R on n=72**, its exact twin **P9-VOLBAR** reads **−0.499R on n=274, −2.97σ**, past the bar at which OMNIGOLD refuses to ticket a mechanic at all. v943 withholds the promotion for exactly that on GOLD SCALP and GOLD SWING. **GOLD ULTRA went on crowning it**, because `crownable` requires `book === 'prefer'` and its prefer book was a **hand-typed copy** of goldind's table — sitting under a comment naming that table as the source. Two desks, one mechanic, one body of evidence, opposite answers. **The prefer book is now READ** (`guPreferKeys()`), so a re-bake moves it with no edit here, and it **fails CLOSED**: with the table unreadable there is no prefer book and nothing is crowned — this tab's whole thesis is that its setups *are* the GOLD SCALP prefer rows, so crowning from a stale local list with the source gone is worse than crowning nothing (hg-v938). The two shown-but-never-crowned extras (`sweepob`, `p8range`) are **not** derived and say so, with the guard asserting each still exists and is not suppressed. **The twin check runs here too, and withholds the CROWN AND NOTHING ELSE** — the setup still forms, keeps its levels, keeps its prefer tier and stays on the board — with the card naming the twin, **both** samples, and the gates and 1h horizon the record belongs to. It **fails OPEN** (a missing verdict is not a negative one) and the read is **live**, so a re-bake that turns the twin positive restores the crown with no edit. **What it costs, said plainly:** on the population this desk forms today `p9volbar` is **50.4% of the prefer book by count** (n=63), and on **this desk's own record** it reads **+0.2268R** net against `p6fail`'s +0.2436R. So half the crownable book loses its crown *and the local record does not say the mechanic is bad*. That is v943's position rather than a new one — refuse to **actively promote** on the smaller of two contradicting records, and change nothing else. `p6fail`'s twin is **not** past the bar (+0.73σ) and it still crowns, so the rule is specific, not blanket. **One lever, not two:** `hgGoldSetTwinCheck(false)` reverses it here as well — and wiring that surfaced a defect in v943's own switch: **there was no reader of it at all, only a resolver.** `hgGoldTwinCheckInit()` re-reads the window override and localStorage and **overwrites** the value in force, so asking it whether the check is on silently discards a `hgGoldSetTwinCheck(false)` wherever localStorage did not take the write — and `goldind.js` is IIFE-wrapped, so `window.HG_GOLD_TWIN_CHECK` is undefined and that is *every* headless harness and every browser with site data blocked. **`hgGoldTwinCheckOn()`** now reads it. **No gate, threshold or verdict moves on any other desk, and no setup leaves any board.** **One consequence worth naming:** fail-closed makes `goldind.js` a real dependency for crowning, and `tests/test-goldultra.mjs` booted the tab **alone** — so it crowned nothing and went red. That is not a brittle guard like the four this session corrected; it is a coupling this pack introduced, and the fix is to boot the desk in the order `index.html` loads it rather than to seed a fake table in the harness, which would be the second copy the pack exists to remove. Tests: `tests/test-gold-ultra-twin-crown.mjs` (35 assertions, **10/10** behavioural mutations caught across two files, baseline verified green first).
- **A measured-positive gold mechanic could not be formed on GOLD SWING at all, and the coverage claim that should have caught it never asked which tab (hg-v945):** Asked again for more gold-specific indicators and strategies on these desks — and the additive well is now **measurably dry**. Ranked by **gate-clear** record at XM, exactly **9 of the 54** measured OMNIGOLD mechanics are net-positive, and after hg-v942 all nine are reachable; the best of the other 45 are `P7-SCALP` **−0.0099R** and `HA-FLIP` **−0.0113R**, falling to `PIN-REJECT` **−0.9396R**, so adding more *measured* mechanics means adding measured losers. **What was missing was not a detector but a tab.** `EQH-SWEEP` is on that derived roster (n=41, **+0.0159R**), its home is **`smcliq`**, GOLD SCALP has minted it since hg-v564 — and **GOLD SWING ran the same detector on its own 4h series and only STAMPED candidates that already existed**, inside a block guarded on `got.length`, so on a quiet tape (exactly when a desk is asked for a setup) an equal-highs sweep produced **nothing** there. hg-v942 called its coverage exhaustive because every roster kind "is ported or has a named home" — **the home check never asked WHICH TAB the home was on**, and a per-kind claim cannot see a per-tab hole. **`hgGoldRosterTabGaps()`** reports it per tab now, and the guard proves the reporter is **not vacuous** by taking the tab back off and requiring the gap to return, and by asserting a kind named on *no* tab is reported against *every* tab rather than skipped. **GOLD SWING mints it:** ENTRY at the swept level (hg-v423), the stop from the **one shared ported-mechanic rule** (`hgGoldRosterStop`) rather than a second invention, and everything downstream is the tab's own — `mkCand` still demands ≥2 agreeing reads and a majority, `push()` still runs the inst filter, the stop-width floor, the edge table, the confluence ledger and the cost gate. It **fails CLOSED**: no detector, no hit, or no shared stop rule and it mints **nothing** rather than reaching for a local re-implementation (hg-v938). It mints **DEMOTED**, and *this* line demotes it, carrying `demotedWhy` that names the record it lacks — a bare `demoted` flag reads the same whichever gate fired (hg-v940), and the desk demotes that row on most tapes anyway, so a guard checking only `demoted` **survived mutation**. **Two defects found while wiring it. (1) The ports were gated on a plan the desk does not trade:** `__swEntryFromZone` **ignores the anchor** when no zone is given and returns the live **mark**, so hg-v942 ran the **1.2R build gate against the mark** and then overwrote entry with the mechanic's level. Driven end to end, a port proposing a **1.500R** plan was **REJECTED at 0.614R**. The five hg-v933/v934 extras set `entry` to the last close, so for *them* mark and entry coincide and nothing was wrong — **the fault was the ports alone**, and the first statement of it here was too broad. **(2) hg-v942 declared the home and never declared the twin**, so `hgGoldSiblingRecord('smcliq')` returned null and the card would have told a reader that "no OMNIGOLD mechanic reads the same thing" while v942's own table named one — the hg-v934 failure exactly. The homes are folded into the twin map **from** the home map so one list decides both (which also puts them under the measured-failure veto), and **`scripts/gold-sibling-records.mjs` reads that same list**, because it had been writing a literal the runtime looked past; the first cut of the fold ran where the home map was still hoisted-undefined and folded in **zero** entries, silently. **Measured, and it moves nothing here:** over **120 scans** of one 4h tape the swing mint is **109 candidates / 204 rejects with and without both changes — identical**; the entry fix changes the R:R the gate computes on ported rows (`ogbosretest` 1.503 → 1.500) without flipping one in or out, and the SMC detector never fires on that tape. **The tape is SYNTHETIC** — every data route is 403 CONNECT — so it is weak evidence of *frequency* and is not offered as more; the behaviour is proved by driving the real `goldSwingSetups`. **Separately, what actually withholds gold setups, measured:** of the **nine** demote stamps GOLD SCALP applies at scale on the **1,205** trades it still forms (hg-v916 population), judged on **four disjoint windows** at **both fill bounds**, exactly **one is supported** — **`EDGE DEMOTE`**, unanimous on win AND gross AND net at both ends (n=824, ΔnetR **−0.137 / −0.152**). The desk's **largest** demote, `CONF NO TRADE` (n=953), **carries no verdict**, and five stamps **lean** the wrong way — the desk marking its *better* rows — without one being unanimous. **A lean is not a verdict**, and the script reports them as separate fields so the distinction cannot be lost in prose. **Nothing is gated on any of it** (hg-v920 refused three loosenings on this evidence; hg-v944 a fourth). Re-derive: `node scripts/demote-separation.mjs`. **A FOURTH guard passing on a fixed-width source slice, caught by this pack's own suite run:** `tests/test-gold-roster-ports.mjs` §11 read the two extras blocks by slicing a fixed **2,200 / 2,600** characters after `hgGoldExtraDetect` and grepping inside; this pack's added lines pushed `demoted = true` past the swing cutoff and turned it red with nothing wrong — the same fragility hg-v944 corrected in §3 of the same file. The slice is now bounded to the **block** (call → its own catch, fatal if either cannot be located) instead of a character count. And the grep could not see **reachability**: wrapping the demote in `if (false)` leaves the text intact and passed, which mutation said out loud — so the check is now **behavioural**, driving the real `goldSwingSetups` and reading the row, with the lever exercised both ways. Both extras blocks carry `demotedWhy` for that reason (one rule, both tabs). **Two more guards pinned the same line's punctuation:** `test-gold-extra-strategies.mjs` and `test-gold-sibling-records.mjs` both asserted the exact text `if (!promo) xCand.demoted = true;`, so attaching an attribution beside that demote turned them red with the rule unchanged. Both now match the **rule** rather than its braces, and the relaxation was proved to be punctuation-only: each still catches the demote being disabled, ignoring the lever, or removed outright (3/3 on both). Four brittle guards in one session — two fixed-width source slices and two exact-line greps — all in the gold tests, all passing or failing for reasons unrelated to what they claim. Tests: `tests/test-gold-swing-smcliq.mjs` (50 assertions) and `tests/test-gold-roster-ports.mjs` (95 assertions), **31/31** behavioural mutations caught across five files, baseline verified green before every pass.
- **The loudest gate on GOLD SCALP is not what empties the board, and the number that shows it is the one nobody had (hg-v944):** `goldind` demotes every setup formed outside an ICT killzone, and a demoted row can never be MOST PROBABLE. On the committed walk that stamp lands on **1,156 of 2,605** formed setups (**44.4%**), and **0 of 111** MOST PROBABLE rows were off-session. Read alone those two figures say session filtering decides the board, and the obvious move is to relax it. **The sole-blocker count says otherwise: OFF-SESSION is the only demote on SIX rows** — 0.5% of those carrying it, **0.2% of the walk** — so relaxing it entirely returns six setups, not 1,156. What actually demotes them: `CONF NO TRADE` 1,504 · `EDGE DEMOTE` 784 · `ASIA SESSION` 567 · `MTF BIAS` 386 · `S56 BALANCE` 223. This is hg-v929's finding in a second place (a share is not a cost; the loud gate is not the binding one) and hg-v184's crypto sole-blocker discipline applied to gold. The first cut of this pack led with the 44.4% share — the sole count was computed only because pulling the lever on the real mint moved **nothing**, and that had to be explained rather than ignored. **Separately, time of day separates nothing:** on four **disjoint** windows at **both** fill bounds, not one killzone split carries a verdict; the cohort the rule most *favours* (London/NY overlap at maximum weight) points the **wrong** way (ΔnetR −0.044 / −0.031, 1 of 4 windows) while the demoted off-session cohort reads mildly **positive** (+0.021 / +0.032, 2 of 4). **So nothing changes, for two independent reasons** — the evidence clears the bar in neither direction, and the rule is not what withholds setups anyway; removing it would be loosening on non-unanimous evidence, which hg-v920 refused three times. `hgGoldSetOffSessionDemote(false)` is the lever and it **defaults to ON**; measured over 120 scans the mint is **identical either way** (173 candidates, 164 demoted, 9 lead-eligible). **Two corrections made in passing:** the killzone weight does **not** feed the grade on the path the walk exercises (recorded grade is `g(tally)` at **100.0%** of 2,283 rows), so the `score = count + kz.weight` line would have a reader believe a lever that is not there; and **GOLD SWING is untouched on purpose** — it states killzones are deliberately not a swing gate, so the desk difference is a documented choice, not drift. **Generated, not transcribed:** `scripts/session-separation.mjs` writes every figure from the replay, the demote-stamp set is **enumerated** so the sole count can be audited, a missing marker is fatal, the tree round-trips to zero drift, the panel renders **nothing** without the literal rather than a zero-filled table, and it **branches** on the verdict so a future bake that finds one is announced. Re-derive: `node scripts/session-separation.mjs [--write]`. **A third guard passing for the wrong reason, found by this pack's own suite run:** `tests/test-gold-roster-ports.mjs` read the two `HG_GOLD_SETUP_EDGE` tables by slicing a **fixed 12,000 characters** after `swing:` and counted `reads:` out of the *next literal in the file* — so it asserted **15** swing keys where the table holds **14**, and had been green on `main` for the wrong reason since hg-v942. Placing `HG_GOLD_SESSION_SEP` beside the table put four more foreign keys inside that window and turned it red, which is how it surfaced. It now reads the **runtime object** out of a loaded `goldind.js` — 27 scalp keys and **14** swing — so no block added nearby can move the count in either direction, and an unreadable table is **fatal** rather than two empty sets quietly passing the collision checks below it. Re-verified 4/4 on genuine add/remove mutations (a swing row removed, a 15th swing row added, a 28th scalp row added, a ported key colliding with an existing mechanic). Tests: `tests/test-session-separation.mjs` (75 assertions, 17/17 mutations caught, baseline green first); `tests/test-gold-roster-ports.mjs` corrected (90 assertions).
- **Two measured records of the same gold mechanic disagree in sign, and the tabs were promoting on the smaller one (hg-v943):** Several mechanics on these tabs are **wired OMNIGOLD mechanics** — the same detector, registered deliberately in hg-v567…v576 — so each carries **two** records, and nothing had compared them. For two they disagree **in sign**, with the tab holding the smaller sample: **`p8range`** (S52 range-bar S0 sweep) tab n=91 **+0.038R** → `neutral` (can lead) vs twin **`P8-RANGE`** n=323 **−0.613R**; **`p9volbar`** (S62 volume-bar sweep) tab n=72 **+0.155R** → **`prefer`** (+2 rank boost) vs twin **`P9-VOLBAR`** n=274 **−0.499R**. Both twins sit past OMNIGOLD's `EDGE_VETO_Z` of **−2**, the bar at which it refuses to ticket a mechanic at all — hg-v934 refused to port `PIVOT-REJECT` on exactly that reasoning and applied it only to a *new* port. **The conservative numbers are the ones quoted:** naive z reads −5.98 / −4.66; OMNIGOLD's own cluster-corrected statistic (`hgOgReplayZ` / `hgOgEffN`, reused not reimplemented) reads **−3.81 / −2.97** on effective n of 131 / 111. Breakeven is **1/3**, because the twin's record was measured on OMNIGOLD's 2R plans, not this desk's 1.5R. **What it does NOT do is the point:** it does **not** demote and does **not** suppress — the two records measure *different populations* (this desk's replay on its own gates and 15m/4h horizons vs the gate-clear population of OMNIGOLD's 35-gate stack on 1h), and importing a foreign population's verdict wholesale is the hg-v923 attribution error, with hg-v920 having measured what acting on a whole-book read does out of sample. **What it does:** refuse to *actively promote* on the smaller of two contradicting records — a `prefer` row whose exact twin is past the bar loses its +2 boost and falls back to neutral; the setup still forms, paints, keeps its levels and can still lead on its own merits. **Every affected card says so**, naming both samples, attributing the foreign record to OMNIGOLD's gates and 1h horizon, stating that neither supersedes the other while naming which way the evidence points, and saying plainly that nothing is demoted on it; a `neutral` row has no boost to withhold, so it discloses and changes nothing. **Loose analogies are not twins** and the guard proves it behaviourally (`nyexh` ≠ NY-OPEN-DRIVE, `liqsweep` ≠ PDL-SWEEP, `silverb` ≠ KZ-JUDAS, `hvn` ≠ FVG-HVN). **Measured on the real mint:** GOLD SWING 111 scans — candidates **unchanged at 72**, boosted rows **22 → 11**, **11 rank boosts withheld**, 22 cards disclosed; GOLD SCALP 120 scans — 173 candidates, 10 boosted, **zero effect on that series** because neither mechanic formed on it (the rule is live on both tabs; the measured bite is on swing). The read is **live, not baked** — a re-bake that turns a twin positive restores the boost with no edit. Fails open; `hgGoldSetTwinCheck(false)` reverses it. **No gate, threshold or verdict moves and no setup leaves either board.** Tests: `tests/test-gold-twin-conflict.mjs` (68 assertions, 12/12 mutations caught, baseline green first).
- **Six mechanics that measured profitable on gold could not be formed on either tab a trader works from (hg-v942):** MILLI GOLD's roster is the nine OMNIGOLD mechanics whose **gate-clear** record is net-positive at XM on ≥ `MIN_SAMPLES` firings — derived, not chosen. **Six of the nine have no mechanic on GOLD SCALP or GOLD SWING at all**: `STRUCT-BOS` (n=87, +0.082R) · `SQUEEZE-FIRE` (n=45, +0.075R) · `CUSUM-SHIFT` (n=32, +0.071R) · `MMOVE` (n=177, +0.059R) · `TREND-RECLAIM` (n=99, +0.055R) · `BOS-RETEST` (n=74, +0.003R). The other three are homed (`P5-DRIVE`→`p5drive`, `P6-COMP`→`p6comp`, `EQH-SWEEP`→`smcliq`). **What is and is not claimed:** a positive in-sample record is not a forecast and this desk measured that directly — hg-v937 rebuilt the same roster on the earlier part of the walk and judged it on what came after, where it beat the full OMNIGOLD book **6 of 6** and **paid 0 of 6**. So the claim is narrow: these are the best-measured mechanics the two tabs lack, and they were absent — not that adding them makes either desk pay. They **mint DEMOTED** (`hgGoldExtraSetPromotable(true)` lifts it), because the record on the card is OMNIGOLD's gates and its 1h horizon. **No second copy:** detection is OMNIGOLD's own **`hgOgBtDetectors()`** dispatch — the same pure `rows → hit|null` functions the live pass and the backtest call — and the guard asserts that with OMNIGOLD absent the block detects **nothing** rather than falling back to a local re-implementation (hg-v938's lesson). **Gating is the tab's, in full:** the hit becomes an ordinary candidate through the inst filter, the stop-width floor, the edge table, the confluence ledger and best-levels. **ENTRY is the level the mechanic named** (hg-v423) and one shared stop rule (`hgGoldRosterStop`: beyond the recent extreme + 0.15×ATR, floored at 0.35×ATR) is stated once instead of invented six times. **A second gap found while wiring it:** GOLD SWING never consumed `hgGoldExtraDetect` at all, so the hg-v933/v934 mechanics (London fix, dollar divergence, round-dollar, weekly open, 61.8 retrace) have never existed on that tab; it consumes them now on its own 4h series, through `push()`. **Coverage is exhaustive by construction** — every roster kind is ported or has a named home, `hgGoldRosterUnmapped()` reports any that is neither, and the roster is **read** from `HG_MILLI_ROSTER` at call time so a re-bake needs no second edit. The **measured-failure veto still runs** on these, proved behaviourally. **Measured on the real mint:** GOLD SCALP 120 scans **108 → 165** candidates, 15 → 21 mechanics, 0 throws, **lead count unchanged at 10**; GOLD SWING 111 scans **52 → 71**, 6 → 9 mechanics, 0 throws, lead count unchanged — the swing desk rejects most of what it is now offered on its own gates (confluence insufficient, R:R under the 1.2R floor). **No gate, threshold or verdict moves.** **The first mutation pass for this pack was worthless and the full suite caught it, not me:** one assertion read the edge tables with a regex demanding exactly one space before the brace and silently got 20 of 27 scalp keys, so the test exited non-zero on every run — and the harness decides *caught* by non-zero exit, so all 14 mutations were false positives. The harness now **refuses a red baseline**; the honest re-run found three real survivors, one of them a defect in this pack's own rule — `num()` coerces, so `+null === 0` and a bar carrying `null` for its low read as a **low of zero** and priced a stop against it (the same trap hg-v941 hit on a null age). `hgGoldRosterPx` now treats a non-number as unreadable and the stop is NaN. Tests: `tests/test-gold-roster-ports.mjs` (89 assertions, 17/17 mutations caught, baseline verified green first).
- **The conviction TTL was unreachable for the records that needed it, and since hg-v930 that is a desk-wide lockout (hg-v941):** `applyHardgateConvictionLock` prices each live record against the latest bar **of its own venue** from `venueRows`, and did `if (!bar) continue;` — while `evaluateSetup` returned null on a missing candle **before** reaching its elapsed/TTL branch. So a record whose venue is absent from the current scan was never evaluated at all: not stopped, not targeted, **not expired**. GOLD SCALP labels its venue from `gold.source`, which walks a feed chain (`xm-xauusd` → `delta-xaut` → `binance-paxg` → proxy), so the moment it resolves to a different label than the one a record was minted under, that record is **orphaned** and outlives its expiry indefinitely. **Proved as a pair:** the same record, the same clock, **400 hours old against a 6-hour TTL** — with its own venue in the scan it **EXPIRES**, with a different venue label it **survives untouched**. **Why it matters now:** before hg-v930 an orphan was a stale history row; since hg-v930/v931 `hgOgOpenGoldConvictions` counts every key in `live` across **both** booking stores and one open conviction **holds every new setup** on GOLD SCALP, GOLD SWING and OMNIGOLD — so one unexpirable record demotes every card on three desks, forever, and the only trace is *"1 gold conviction is live"*, which is what a position someone just took looks like. **The fix is the TTL and nothing else:** the elapsed check is the one invalidation that needs no price, so it runs **before** the candle guard and the pass no longer skips a record it cannot price. Every price-based check still **requires** a bar — pricing a record against another venue's candles is a worse repair, not a stricter one, and the guard asserts a stop-breaching bar under the **wrong** venue does not stop it; a fresh orphan inside its TTL still stands. **Plus disclosure, because a bare count is how this hid:** `hgOgOpenGoldConvictions` returns `rows` (desk · symbol · direction · venue · **age**) and **`hgOgHoldingRowsHtml`** names each holding record on both panels, GOLD SCALP delegating to the one definition; a record whose store never wrote an `issuedAt` reads **age not recorded**, never `0m` (the first cut used `isFinite(+null)`, which is 0 — the guard caught it). **No gate, threshold or verdict moves.** Tests: `tests/test-conviction-orphan-expiry.mjs`.
- **Suppress/demote/prefer verdicts HAND-TUNED, on instruction (hg-v928):** **No new evidence supports any of it and none is claimed** — hg-v916 refused one of these exact flips. The tuning is **mechanical**: the table's own bars (`suppress` n≥50 ∧ gross≤0 ∧ netXm≤−0.20; `demote` netXm<0; `prefer` n≥50 ∧ gross>0 ∧ netXm≥+0.10, **all unchanged**) applied to the population the desk still forms instead of the whole 2,193-trade book, 45% of which it doesn't. **Four verdicts move, all loosening:** `nyexh` suppress→demote (live −0.183R, n=117), `liqsweep` suppress→demote (−0.048R, n=54, gross **positive**), `bosalign` demote→neutral (+0.013R, n=125), `ribbon` demote→neutral (+0.027R, n=102).
